There are $k$ inverse scalar factors here, versus $k+1$ after division by $H_i$. The delay begins at $\eps^{3q}$, so at each new stage it adds only known lower-order forcing. The polynomial inverse \eqref{air:ka:eq:polyinv} remains unchanged. Rational denominators stay bounded away from zero on the cutoff; there are only finitely many additional fixed shifts. The previous arbitrary-order remainder proof therefore applies in full, with the same exact bottom zeros and common normalization.

At first altered order the DFA subtraction is $q^k/(2k)\eps^{3q}f$, while the compacted value is twice this. It is absorbed solely into the scalar, giving
\[
 \sigma_{3q}^B-\sigma_{3q}^R=-\frac{q^k}{2k},\qquad
 \sigma_{3q}^C-\sigma_{3q}^R=-\frac{q^k}{k}.
\]
Earlier profiles agree, and the new profile at this first altered stage agrees too. Since $h_{3q-3}$ enters the scalar logarithm with coefficient $-(q-1)$, the first DFA change in it is $q^k/[2k^2(q-1)]$, with twice this for compacted trees. At $i=kn$ this proves \eqref{air:ka:eq:comp-ratio}--\eqref{air:ka:eq:dfa-ratio}.

The positive first correction implies approach from above. For strict decrease, use enough finite terms to leave a remainder $o(n^{-(k-1)})$ in the logarithmic ratio. Subtract its values at $n+1$ and $n$. The leading difference is $-(k-2)A n^{-(k-1)}$ with $A>0$; all higher retained powers and the difference of the two remainders are smaller. This avoids differentiating an uncontrolled asymptotic remainder.

\subsection{Cancellation between the two ratios}
Let $u_n=C_n/R_n$ and $v_n=B_n/(2^{n-1}R_n)$, with positive limits $u_\infty=\rho_C$ and $v_\infty=\rho_B$. A stronger cancellation holds:
\begin{equation}\label{air:ka:eq:cancellation}
 \log\frac{u_n}{u_\infty}-2\log\frac{v_n}{v_\infty}
 =-\frac{q^q}{k^k}n^{-q}+O_k(n^{-q-1/3}).
\end{equation}
Every preceding fractional power vanishes. In particular the coefficient is $-4/27$ for $k=3$ and $-27/256$ for $k=4$.

Here is the order argument that controls nonlinear feedback. Put $d=3q\ge6$ and compare each formal solution to the relaxed one in the same boundary gauge. The first scalar change has order $\eps^d$ and is pure $f$ forcing, so its profile correction is zero. Thus the individual profile changes begin at order $\eps^{d-1}$, and the inverse scalar-product changes begin at order $\eps^d$. Every nonlinear response product has order at least $2d-1>d+3$. Through order $d+3$ the response is therefore genuinely linear.

The exact coefficient identity is
\[
 \beta^C-2\beta^B=-\frac{q^{2k}}{(X)_k}
 =-q^kk^k\eps^{d+3}+O(\eps^{d+5}).
\]
After the $k$ inverse scalar factors, the combined forcing is $+q^k\eps^{d+3}f$. The polynomial inverse therefore gives
\[
 \sigma^C-2\sigma^B+\sigma^R
 =q^k\eps^{d+3}+O(\eps^{d+4}),
\]
with zero combined profiles through index $d+1$. Taking scalar logarithms introduces nonlinear terms only at order $2d$, still too late. Scalar matching then gives the combined coefficient
$h_d^C-2h_d^B+h_d^R=-q^{k-1}/k$. The endpoint logarithms contribute nothing through order $\eps^d$, since all profiles vanish at zero and their combined correction starts at index $d+2$. Evaluation at $i=kn$ gives \eqref{air:ka:eq:cancellation}; the all-finite-orders theorem supplies its remainder with the same amplitudes.

\section{Inverse counting scales and integer thresholds}\label{air:ka:sec:inverse}
\begin{writenote}
The Lambert anchor and the threshold rule are instances of theorems \repopath{p0:thm:lambert-core}, \repopath{p0:thm:perturbed-inversion} and \repopath{p0:thm:staircase} of the repository's transseries volume (Part~0). No novelty is claimed for the inversion mechanics.
\end{writenote}

This section applies separately to any of the three models. Write its expansion as
\[
 T_n=C(n!)^q A^n e^{\gamma n^{1/3}}n^\eta
 \left(1+\sum_{r=1}^Mc_rn^{-r/3}+O(n^{-(M+1)/3})\right),
\]
where $A=k^k/q^q$ for relaxed or compacted trees and $A=2k^k/q^q$ for DFAs. The amplitude $C$ is the corresponding positive model constant. Set
\[
 \nu=\frac q2+\eta=\frac{7k-5}{6},\qquad
 d=\log C+\frac q2\log(2\pi),\qquad L=\log y.
\]
Let $x(y)$ denote the inverse of the \emph{log-linearly interpolated exact counts}: between consecutive integers interpolate $\log T_n$, then invert that eventually increasing function. With the principal Lambert function $W_0$, define
\[
 w=W_0\left(\frac{A^{1/q}L}{qe}\right),\qquad
 n_0=\frac{L}{qw},\qquad D=q(w+1).
\]
Then
\begin{equation}\label{air:ka:eq:inverse}
 x(y)=n_0-\frac{\gamma n_0^{1/3}+\nu\log n_0+d}{D}
 +o\left(\frac1{\log n_0}\right).
\end{equation}
An equivalent form displaying the constant displacement is
\[
 x(y)=n_0-\frac{\gamma n_0^{1/3}}D-\frac\nu q
 -\frac{d-(\nu/q)\log A}{D}
 +o\left(\frac1{\log n_0}\right).
\]
Indeed $n_0$ solves $qn_0\log n_0+(\log A-q)n_0=L$, and the derivative of this leading expression is $D$. Inserting the displayed correction leaves logarithmic residual $O(n_0^{-1/3})=o(1)$, including the omitted first finite-order term; the actual chord slopes are asymptotic to $q\log n_0$. Division by the slope proves \eqref{air:ka:eq:inverse}.

For arbitrary finite inverse accuracy, take a sufficiently long smooth logarithmic truncation $H_M(t)$ of the proven expansion, interpolate its integer values linearly, and invert that interpolation. At nearby integer sites the logarithmic error is $O(n^{-(M+1)/3})$, which interpolation preserves. The inverse error is therefore
\[
 O\left(\frac{n_0^{-(M+1)/3}}{\log n_0}\right).
\]
This is an effective all-finite-orders inversion procedure once the amplitude is specified; it does not numerically determine the amplitude.

A smooth inverse alone omits an oscillatory lattice correction. If $H(z)=\log y$ for a sufficiently accurate smooth logarithmic truncation and $\theta=\{z\}$, the first smooth-to-chord inverse correction is
\[
 -\frac{q\theta(1-\theta)}{2zH'(z)}
 +o\left(\frac1{z\log z}\right).
\]
This follows from the chord excess $H''(z)\theta(1-\theta)/2$, with $H''(z)\sim q/z$. Finally, for large $y$, the exact threshold
\[
 N(y)=\min\{n:T_n\ge y\}=\lceil x(y)\rceil.
\]
It need not equal the ceiling of a finite approximation to $x(y)$ when that approximation is too close to an integer.

\section{Checks scope and further research}\label{air:ka:sec:checks}
The independent verification chain separates analytic proofs from finite calculations. The exact fixed-weight and phase identities pass 2,095 rational checks for $k=3,\ldots,11$. The source seeds, completed-run sums, and signed transforms pass 2,403 rational checks for $k=3,\ldots,8$ and $n\le7$. A separate signed-model replay passes 11,016 independent checks. The first two relaxed coefficients were independently recomputed by a separate symbolic direct-linear-system method through order $\eps^5$. These checks guard algebra and indexing; compactness, tracking, and error transfer have separate proofs.

The only external asymptotic growth input needed for positivity is the relaxed lower bound in Dastidar--Wallner \cite[Section~3.4, equation~(9)]{gdw}. It occurs before the paper's subsequent high-path upper-bound argument. In particular, the present signed-delay route does not use a pointwise bound $U_i\le q$, which is false at some active bottom edges, and needs no bridge-tail repair. The valid bound used here is $U_i(j)\le q\alpha_i$ for active $j\ge1$.
\begin{writenote}
That the published proof uses a false bound $U_i\le q$ is source 18's statement (and source 66's, Section~\ref{air:t3:subsec:hightail}); this report has not checked it against the paper. See Part~0, ``A correction of the literature that this report has not verified''.
\end{writenote}


The following remain distinct questions rather than consequences of this report.
\begin{enumerate}
\item \textbf{Explicit amplitude evaluation.} Construct a certified convergent numerical method with rigorous tail bounds for $A_k^R,A_k^C,A_k^B$, or obtain a structural representation that evaluates them. Positivity alone does not provide their values.
\item \textbf{Large-order control.} Determine the growth of the recursively generated coefficients, possible Gevrey bounds, optimal truncation, and exponentially small remainders. All finite orders do not by themselves imply an exponentially accurate or transseries completion.
\item \textbf{Growing arity.} Study whether a simultaneous $k,n$ regime exists. The fixed-weight roots, spectral constants, and remainder bounds here may deteriorate with $k$; no uniformity has been proved.
\item \textbf{Monotonicity at every size.} Are $C_n/R_n$ and $B_n/(2^{n-1}R_n)$ nonincreasing for every $n$ and fixed $k\ge3$? The theorem proves only eventual decrease. The positive completed-run formula alone does not couple the bridge measures at different terminal sizes.
\item \textbf{Further model comparisons.} Beyond the cancellation in \eqref{air:ka:eq:cancellation}, determine additional universal combinations and their higher corrections. At sufficiently high orders the response becomes nonlinear, so further cancellation cannot be assumed from the first-order defect alone.
\item \textbf{Sharper endpoint errors.} Boundary-adapted resolvent or pointwise estimates may reduce the $i^{1/2}$ loss in norm-to-endpoint extraction. This could improve efficient rigorous numerical evaluation even though arbitrary finite orders are already available.
\end{enumerate}

\addtocontents{toc}{\protect\smallskip\protect\noindent\textit{The ternary case (source 66)}\protect\par}
\bigskip\noindent{\Large\bfseries The ternary case (source 66)}\par\nopagebreak
\begin{partsource}
Batch-77 manuscript 66, archive \repopath{ternary-airy-amplitudes-result.zip}, main file \repopath{report/ternary-airy-amplitudes.tex} (656 lines; 14-page PDF), titled ``Airy amplitudes for ternary trees and finite language automata'', author line ``Research report'', dated 2 October 2026. Printed in full as the ternary section of Part~\ref{air:part:ka}, label prefix \texttt{air:t3:}.
\end{partsource}

\paragraph{Abstract of source 66.}
\begin{quote}\small
We prove positive limiting amplitudes for relaxed ternary trees and for minimal deterministic finite automata accepting finite languages over a three-letter alphabet. For relaxed trees we also construct an asymptotic expansion to every fixed finite order. The proof uses a fixed weighted Hilbert norm that matches the forward and adjoint boundary harmonics, a compact singular Airy limit, and evolving polynomial--Airy profiles. A corrected uniform bridge-tail estimate transfers the relaxed amplitude to the automata. The leading scales were established or motivated in earlier work; the results here strengthen the relaxed Theta estimate to an equivalent and transfer an equivalent to the ternary automata. No all-orders automaton expansion or general-alphabet theorem is asserted.
\end{quote}


\begin{writenote}
Source 66 is the case $k=3$ of source 18 and the base proof that source 18 generalizes (see the note at the head of this Part). Its Theorems~\ref{air:t3:thm:main} and~\ref{air:t3:thm:orders} are the case $k=3$ of Theorems~\ref{air:ka:thm:all} and~\ref{air:ka:thm:ratio}: $R_n$ and $B_n$ are Part~\ref{air:part:ka}'s counts at $k=3$, $C_R=A_3^R$, $C_B=A_3^B$, $\rho=\rho_B$, and $P_3$ is the product $P_B$ of \eqref{air:ka:eq:products} at $q=2$, by Euler's sine product. They are kept in source 66's normalization because the proof that follows is that base proof; nothing in them is a separate result. Source 66's own additions are the third coefficient $c_3$ and the scalar coefficients $\mathfrak s_3,\ldots,\mathfrak s_6$; the explicit ternary weight of Lemma~\ref{air:t3:lem:harmonic}, with $1\le\omega\le3/2$ (Lemma~\ref{air:ka:lem:weight} gives $1\le\omega\le q$); and the bridge-tail route to the automaton amplitude of Section~\ref{air:t3:sec:defects}, a second route beside source 18's signed-delay route. Its statements ``no general-alphabet theorem'' and ``no all-orders automaton expansion'' are about source 66 alone: source 18 proves both for every $k\ge3$.
\end{writenote}

\section{Statements and normalizations}\label{air:t3:sec:statements}
Let $R_n$ count relaxed ternary trees with $n$ internal nodes, including $R_0=1$. This is the sequence \href{https://oeis.org/A082162}{A082162}, beginning
\[
1,1,7,139,5711,408354,45605881,\ldots.
\]
Let $B_n$ count minimal DFAs accepting finite languages on a fixed three-letter alphabet, with $n$ transient states and one rejecting sink. Thus $B_0=1$ and
\[
B_1=1,\quad B_2=14,\quad B_3=532,\quad B_4=42644.
\]
These are different counting sequences. In particular, A082162 is not the minimal finite-language DFA sequence.

Write $a=a_1<0$ for the largest real zero of $\Ai$, and put
\[
\gamma=3\,3^{1/3}a.
\]
The published relaxed-tree theorem of Dastidar and Wallner \cite{gdw} gives the Theta scale used below, including its positive lower bound. Our amplitude and finite-order conclusions are additional arguments, not statements attributed to that paper.

\begin{theorem}[Positive amplitudes; the case $k=3$]\label{air:t3:thm:main}
There are constants $C_R,C_B>0$ such that
\begin{align}
R_n&\sim C_R(n!)^2\left(\frac{27}{4}\right)^n
 e^{\gamma n^{1/3}}n^{5/3},\label{air:t3:eq:Rmain}\\
B_n&\sim C_B(n!)^2\left(\frac{27}{2}\right)^n
 e^{\gamma n^{1/3}}n^{5/3}.\label{air:t3:eq:Bmain}
\end{align}
Moreover,
\[
\frac{B_n}{2^{n-1}R_n}\longrightarrow\rho\in[P_3,1],\qquad
P_3=\frac{2\sqrt2}{\pi}\sin\frac{\pi}{\sqrt2}>0,
\qquad C_B=\frac{\rho C_R}{2}.
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Every fixed finite order for relaxed trees; the case $k=3$]\label{air:t3:thm:orders}
For every integer $M\ge0$ there are computable constants $c_1,\ldots,c_M$ such that
\[
R_n=C_R(n!)^2\left(\frac{27}{4}\right)^n e^{\gamma n^{1/3}}n^{5/3}
\left(1+\sum_{r=1}^{M}c_rn^{-r/3}+O\bigl(n^{-(M+1)/3}\bigr)\right).
\]
The same positive $C_R$ works for every $M$. The assertion is an asymptotic expansion to each fixed finite order, not convergence of an infinite series.
\end{theorem}

The first coefficients, in exactly this normalization, are
\begin{align*}
c_1&=3^{-1/3}\frac{89a^2}{90},\\
c_2&=3^{-2/3}\left(\frac{7921a^4}{16200}+\frac{115a}{27}\right),\\
c_3&=\frac{704969a^6}{13122000}+\frac{115931a^3}{85050}
       +\frac{1514}{945}.
\end{align*}
The recursion and remainder argument, rather than finite symbolic checks alone, justify these coefficients. Neither theorem determines the leading constants in closed form.
\begin{writenote}
$c_1$ and $c_2$ are the $k=3$ values displayed after Theorem~\ref{air:ka:thm:ratio}; $c_3$ is source 66's own. A numerical check made at intake (exact $R_n$ for $n\le640$; not shipped) is consistent with $c_3$: with it the successive differences of the corrected amplitude shrink at the rate $n^{-4/3}$ predicts, without it at the rate $n^{-1}$.
\end{writenote}


\section{Exact transfer and the boundary weight}
The relaxed array has support $x\ge2m\ge0$ and satisfies
\[
r_{x,m}=r_{x,m-1}+(m+1)r_{x-1,m},\qquad r_{x,0}=1.
\]
Set $i=x+m$, $j=x-2m$, and use the exact normalization
\[
d_i(j)=\frac{4^x}{x!}r_{x,m}.
\]
This gauge multiplies every path to a fixed endpoint by the same positive factor, so normalized bridge measures are unchanged. The resulting array satisfies
\begin{align}
d_i(j)&=U_i(j)d_{i-1}(j-1)+d_{i-1}(j+2),\label{air:t3:eq:transfer}\\
U_i(j)&=\frac{4(i-j+3)}{2i+j}
      =2-v_i(j),\qquad v_i(j)=\frac{6(j-2)}{2i+j},\label{air:t3:eq:U}\\
R_n&=\frac{(2n)!}{16^n}\,d_{3n}(0).\label{air:t3:eq:factorial}
\end{align}
Here $d_0(0)=1$ and all missing coordinates are zero. At time $i$ the physical space is
\[
J_i=\{j:0\le j\le i,\ j\equiv i\pmod3\}.
\]
The rectangular transfer $T_i$ maps the space on $J_{i-1}$ to that on $J_i$.

The critical infinite-phase transfer is
\[
(Tu)(j)=2u(j-1)+u(j+2),\qquad j\ge0.
\]
It is not diagonally symmetrizable: its two directed jumps have unequal lengths. The following fixed weight is the useful replacement for a self-adjoint transfer.

\begin{lemma}[Compatible forward and adjoint harmonics]\label{air:t3:lem:harmonic}
Define
\[
h(j)=j+1,\qquad
\ell(j)=j+\frac43+\frac16\left(-\frac12\right)^j,\qquad
\omega(j)=\frac{\ell(j)}{h(j)},\qquad \pi(j)=\ell(j)h(j).
\]
Then $1\le\omega(j)\le3/2$ and
\[
Th=3h,\qquad T^*\ell=3\ell,
\]
where $\ell(-1)=\ell(-2)=0$. In the fixed phase norms
$\norm{u}_\omega^2=\sum_j\omega(j)|u(j)|^2$, one has $\norm{T}\le3$.
If $g(j)=u(j)/h(j)$ and the input is finitely supported, then
\begin{equation}\label{air:t3:eq:critical-energy}
9\norm{u}_\omega^2-\norm{Tu}_\omega^2
=\sum_{\substack{j\ge1\\j\text{ in output phase}}}
2\omega(j)j(j+3)|g(j-1)-g(j+2)|^2.
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Substitute the formulas into the two recurrences. In particular the adjoint bottom equations are $2\ell(1)=3\ell(0)$ and $2\ell(2)=3\ell(1)$; the geometric term is essential there. The $h$-conjugate of $T/3$ has row probabilities
\[
p_j=\frac{2j}{3(j+1)},\qquad q_j=\frac{j+3}{3(j+1)}.
\]
At $j=0$, only the second term remains and equals one. The adjoint identity makes $\pi$ invariant between successive residue phases. Jensen's inequality proves the contraction. Its exact two-point variance identity gives \eqref{air:t3:eq:critical-energy}, including the last edge from the input support to its zero extension.
\end{proof}

The weighted adjoint $T^\dagger=\omega^{-1}T^*\omega$ also sends $h$ to $3h$. Thus both sides have the same boundary harmonic profile. The unweighted singular profiles, by contrast, have residue-dependent extrapolation lengths; ignoring that mismatch does not prove an amplitude theorem.
\begin{writenote}
This weight is Lemma~\ref{air:ka:lem:weight} at $k=3$: there $\sum_jl_jz^j=3/(2-3z+z^3)$, whose coefficients are $l_j=j+\frac43+\frac16(-\frac12)^j$. It is the ``matched forward and adjoint boundary profiles'' that source 29 asks for in Section~\ref{air:ka:al:sec:open}.
\end{writenote}


\section{The singular Airy limit}
Put $\alpha_i=1+3/(2i+1)$ and conjugate the finite transfer by $h$:
\[
Q_i(j,k)=\frac{T_i(j,k)h(k)}{3\alpha_i h(j)},\quad
r_i(j)=\sum_kQ_i(j,k),\quad
c_i(k)=\frac{1}{\pi(k)}\sum_j\pi(j)Q_i(j,k).
\]
Since $U_i(j)\le2\alpha_i$ on active up edges, both $r_i$ and $c_i$ are at most one. The input column loss is exactly
\begin{equation}\label{air:t3:eq:column-loss}
1-c_i(k)=\frac{\alpha_i-1}{\alpha_i}
+\frac{v_i(k+1)\ell(k+1)}{3\alpha_i\ell(k)}.
\end{equation}
It vanishes at $k=0$ and is at least $c k/i$ for $k\ge1$, with an absolute $c>0$. One may take $c=4/9$ for all physical input sites. There is no missing upper column correction: both possible nonnegative output rows are present.

For each row write $p=Q_i(j,j-1)$, $q=Q_i(j,j+2)$, assigning zero to a missing input. The following decomposition is exact:
\begin{align}
9\alpha_i^2\norm{u}_\omega^2-\norm{T_i u}_\omega^2
=9\alpha_i^2\bigg\{&\sum_k\pi(k)(1-c_i(k))|g(k)|^2\notag\\
&+\sum_j\pi(j)pq|g(j-1)-g(j+2)|^2\label{air:t3:eq:positiveform}\\
&+\sum_j\pi(j)(1-r_i(j))
[p|g(j-1)|^2+q|g(j+2)|^2]\bigg\}.\notag
\end{align}
It follows by summing the column masses and applying
\[
p|a|^2+q|b|^2-|pa+qb|^2
=pq|a-b|^2+(1-p-q)(p|a|^2+q|b|^2).
\]
Every term in \eqref{air:t3:eq:positiveform} is nonnegative.

\begin{lemma}[Compact Airy limit]\label{air:t3:lem:singular}
For each fixed $m\ge1$, the $m$th largest singular value of $T_i$ in the fixed weighted norms satisfies
\[
\sigma_{i,m}=3\left(1+a_m i^{-2/3}+o(i^{-2/3})\right),
\]
where $a_1>a_2>\cdots$ are the negative Airy zeros. The normalized first right singular vector tends, under mesh-$3i^{-1/3}$ interpolation, to the positive normalized $\Ai(a_1+x)$ on $\R_+$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Let $\eps=i^{-1/3}$ and consider the self-adjoint singular forms
\[
\mathcal E_i(u)=\frac{9\norm{u}_\omega^2-\norm{T_i u}_\omega^2}{18\eps^2}.
\]
Replacing $9$ by $9\alpha_i^2$ changes this form by $O(\eps)\norm{u}_\omega^2$. In particular the forms are bounded below uniformly. We give the compactness and limiting constants explicitly.

On an input phase $r$, let $H_k=r+3k+1$ and write $u_k=u(r+3k)$. Direct telescoping gives
\begin{equation}\label{air:t3:eq:radial}
\sum_{k\ge0}H_kH_{k+1}
\left|\frac{u_{k+1}}{H_{k+1}}-\frac{u_k}{H_k}\right|^2
=\sum_{k\ge0}|u_{k+1}-u_k|^2+\frac{3|u_0|^2}{r+1}.
\end{equation}
The input is extended by zero at infinity. If $F_i$ is linear between the nodes
\[
x_k=\eps H_k,\qquad F_i(x_k)=\frac{u_k}{\sqrt{3\eps}},
\qquad F_i(0)=0,
\]
then the right side of \eqref{air:t3:eq:radial}, divided by $9\eps^2$, is exactly $\int|F_i'|^2$.

The moving variance sum omits the critical top edge to zero. Therefore localize first: multiply $u_k$ by a cutoff equal to one for $x_k\le R$ and zero for $x_k\ge2R$. For fixed $R$ and large $i$, these edges are far below the physical top and $U_i$ is bounded below. Writing $\mathcal Q$ for the radial sum in \eqref{air:t3:eq:radial}, product differences and $H_{k+1}/H_k\le4$ give
\[
\mathcal Q(\chi_Ru)\le C\mathcal Q(u; x\le2R+3\eps)
+C\eps^2R^{-2}\norm{u}^2.
\] Hence bounded norms and bounded $\mathcal E_i$ imply uniform local $H^1$ bounds for the zero-anchored interpolants. In particular
\[
\int_0^\delta|F_i|^2\le\frac{\delta^2}{2}\int_0^\delta|F_i'|^2,
\]
and the corresponding nodal mass is $O(\delta^2+\eps^2)$. The lower endpoint cannot carry concentrated mass.

The column loss \eqref{air:t3:eq:column-loss} also yields
\begin{equation}\label{air:t3:eq:tail-tight}
\sum_{\eps k\ge R}\omega(k)|u(k)|^2\le C/R
\end{equation}
for bounded form energy. Local compactness, the lower-end estimate and \eqref{air:t3:eq:tail-tight} give global strong $L^2$ compactness. Weighted nodal norms, unweighted nodal norms and interpolated norms have the same limit: away from zero $\omega\to1$, and the two tails are uniformly negligible. The same holds for inner products.

On any compact interval $0<\delta<x<R$, the variance term in \eqref{air:t3:eq:positiveform} converges to
$\int x^2|(F/x)'|^2$. Each loss term contributes one half of $\int x|F|^2$. Indeed $v_i(j)=3x\eps^2+O(\eps^3)$ there, and both normalized row and column losses equal $x\eps^2+O(\eps^3)$. Local strong $L^2$ convergence and the gradient bound justify translated sampling. Discarding the nonnegative terms outside $[\delta,R]$, then sending $\delta\downarrow0$ and $R\uparrow\infty$, gives the liminf form
\[
\mathcal E(F)=\int_0^\infty\bigl(|F'|^2+x|F|^2\bigr)\,dx,
\qquad F\in H^1_0(\R_+).
\]
Here $\int x^2|(F/x)'|^2=\int|F'|^2$ by Hardy's inequality and integration by parts. The zero trace was supplied by \eqref{air:t3:eq:radial}; no Neumann or Robin boundary has been introduced.

For recovery sequences sample smooth compactly supported functions inside $(0,\infty)$ as $u_k=\sqrt{3\eps}F(x_k)$. Direct Riemann sums give the norm and form limits. Such functions form a core of the displayed form. Finite-dimensional recovery spaces give the min--max upper bounds. For the lower bounds, compactness of the first $m$ discrete eigenvectors preserves their orthonormality and gives an $m$-dimensional limiting subspace; apply the liminf to its linear combinations. The limiting operator is $-\partial_x^2+x$ with Dirichlet boundary, with eigenvalues $-a_m$. Thus
\[
\frac{9-\sigma_{i,m}^2}{18\eps^2}\longrightarrow-a_m.
\]
Simplicity of the first limit eigenvalue gives ground-vector convergence. The first discrete vector can be chosen positive because $T_i^\dagger T_i$ is irreducible on its input residue chain.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}
The lemma concerns one occupied residue channel. On the direct sum of all three channels, the first three singular values have the same leading Airy level. It would be incorrect to use $a_2$ as the second level on that full space.
\end{remark}

\section{An evolving Airy profile}
Let $f(x)=\Ai(a+x)$ and
\[
g(x)=\left(-\frac{5x^2}{12}-\frac{ax}{6}\right)f(x).
\]
Choose a fixed smooth cutoff shape, equal to one up to one and zero past two, and apply it at $x/\log i$. With $\eps=i^{-1/3}$ define
\begin{align*}
F_i(j)&=\chi_i\bigl(\eps(j+1)\bigr)
\left[f\bigl(\eps(j+1)\bigr)+\eps g\bigl(\eps(j+1)\bigr)\right],\\
N_i&=\norm{F_i}_\omega,\qquad \psi_i=F_i/N_i,\\
t_i&=3\left(1+a i^{-2/3}+\frac{5}{2i}\right),\qquad
s_i=t_i\frac{N_i}{N_{i-1}}.
\end{align*}
All estimates from this point begin at one fixed sufficiently large time $I$. Earlier evolution is absorbed into its finite initial vector.

\begin{lemma}[Two-sided evolving residual]\label{air:t3:lem:residual}
Uniformly through all three phases,
\begin{align}
\norm{T_i\psi_{i-1}/s_i-\psi_i}_\omega&=O(i^{-4/3}),\label{air:t3:eq:forwardres}\\
\norm{T_i^\dagger\psi_i/s_i-\psi_{i-1}}_\omega&=O(i^{-1}).\label{air:t3:eq:adjres}
\end{align}
Also $N_i\asymp i^{1/6}$, $N_i/N_{i-1}=1+O(i^{-1})$, and, when $i\equiv0\pmod3$, $\psi_i(0)\asymp i^{-1/2}$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The profile is evolving, not a frozen eigenvector. In particular
$(i-1)^{-1/3}=\eps(1+\eps^3/3+O(\eps^6))$ must be retained.
At $x=\eps(j+1)$, Taylor expansion of the uncut $T_iF_{i-1}$ gives
\begin{align*}
3f+3\eps g+3\eps^2(f''-xf)
+\eps^3\{f'''+4xf'+9f+3(g''-xg)\}+O(\eps^4).
\end{align*}
The $4xf'$ includes $xf'$ from the time dilation. Using $f''=(x+a)f$,
\[
f'''+4xf'+9f=10f+(5x+a)f',
\]
while
\[
3\{g''-(x+a)g\}=-\frac52f-(5x+a)f'.
\]
The result is $t_i(f+\eps g)+O(\eps^4)$. The remainder has a square-integrable polynomial--Airy envelope, uniformly on the moving support. A mesh sum costs $O(\eps^{-1})$ in squared norm, canceled by $N_i^2\asymp\eps^{-1}$. The lower missing input corresponds exactly to $x=0$, where $f=g=0$. This proves \eqref{air:t3:eq:forwardres} after normalization.

We spell out the adjoint boundary estimate, where an unweighted argument would fail. The exact weighted adjoint is
\[
(T_i^\dagger u)(t)=
\frac{U_i(t+1)\omega(t+1)u(t+1)+\omega(t-2)u(t-2)}{\omega(t)}.
\]
Let $B=\omega^{-1}T^*\omega$ be the critical weighted adjoint, and let $T^*$ here denote its unweighted counterpart. For $t\ge2$ put
\[
a_t=\frac{2(\omega(t+1)-\omega(t))}{\omega(t)}.
\]
Both $B$ and $T^*$ send $h$ to $3h$ at those sites. Hence, exactly,
\begin{equation}\label{air:t3:eq:commutator}
[(B-T^*)F](t)=a_t h(t+1)
\left[\frac{F(t+1)}{h(t+1)}-\frac{F(t-2)}{h(t-2)}\right].
\end{equation}
Its coefficient is bounded by $C((t+1)^{-1}+2^{-t})$. Write
$F(t)/h(t)=\eps\Phi_\eps(\eps(t+1))$, where
$\Phi_\eps(x)=(f(x)+\eps g(x))/x$.
For $0\le x\le1$,
$|\Phi_\eps'(x)|\le C(x+\eps)$ because $f(0)=f''(0)=0$.
Thus \eqref{air:t3:eq:commutator} is $O(\eps^3)$ per site near the bottom. Above the Airy scale, the same estimate has an integrable polynomial--Airy envelope. The operator $T^*-T$ is a third finite difference and has the same order.

At the exceptional sites check directly:
\[
(BF)(0)=\tfrac32F(1),\quad (BF)(1)=2F(2),\qquad
(TF)(0)=F(2),\quad (TF)(1)=2F(0)+F(3).
\]
The linear terms cancel, and the quadratic term of $f$ vanishes. The remaining terms are $O(\eps^3)$. The difference of the adjoint and forward inhomogeneous perturbations is
\[
v_i(t)F(t-1)-v_i(t+1)\frac{\omega(t+1)}{\omega(t)}F(t+1).
\]
A first-difference estimate gives normalized norm $O(\eps^3)$; at fixed $t$ it is $O(\eps^4)$. Replacing $F_i$ by $F_{i-1}$ has normalized cost $O(\eps^3)$ by time dilation. These facts prove \eqref{air:t3:eq:adjres}.

All cutoff discrepancies occur at $x\ge\log i-O(\eps)$, where the Airy envelope is bounded by a polynomial times $e^{-c(\log i)^{3/2}}$. They are smaller than every fixed power of $i$.

Finally, Euler--Maclaurin on a residue phase $r$ gives
\begin{equation}\label{air:t3:eq:normexpansion}
N_{r,\eps}^2=C_{-1}\eps^{-1}+C_0+C_1\eps+O(\eps^2),
\qquad C_{-1}=\tfrac13\int_0^\infty f^2>0,
\end{equation}
with the first three constants independent of $r$. To see the phase order, set $H_\eps=(f+\eps g)^2$. Then $H_\eps(0)=H_\eps'(0)=0$; ordinary phase-sum endpoint corrections begin at $O(\eps^2)$. The algebraic weight contributes $\eps H_\eps(x)/(3x)$, whose quotient vanishes at zero, and its first phase-dependent correction is again $O(\eps^2)$. The geometric part of $\omega$ is $O(\eps^2)$ in the sum. Formula \eqref{air:t3:eq:normexpansion} proves the adjacent-time ratio, including the change of phase. The endpoint estimate follows from $f(\eps)=\Ai'(a)\eps+O(\eps^3)$.
\end{proof}

\section{Tracking and the relaxed amplitude}
Normalize the evolution by $A_i=T_i/s_i$ and decompose an exact solution as
\[
y_i=A_i y_{i-1}=b_i\psi_i+z_i,\qquad z_i\perp\psi_i.
\]
The two residuals give the block estimates
\begin{align}
b_i&=(1+O(i^{-4/3}))b_{i-1}+\beta_i z_{i-1},
&\norm{\beta_i}&=O(i^{-1}),\label{air:t3:eq:blocks1}\\
z_i&=v_i b_{i-1}+D_i z_{i-1},
&\norm{v_i}&=O(i^{-4/3}).\label{air:t3:eq:blocks2}
\end{align}
The vector symbol $v_i$ in this section is unrelated to the scalar up-weight loss in \eqref{air:t3:eq:U}.

\begin{lemma}[Compressed contraction]\label{air:t3:lem:compression}
For some $c>0$ and all sufficiently large $i$,
$\norm{D_i}\le1-ci^{-2/3}$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The explicit input profile converges to the same Airy ground function as the first right singular vector, so its squared overlap is $1-o(1)$. If $x\perp\psi_{i-1}$, singular decomposition gives
\[
\norm{T_i x}^2\le
[\sigma_{i,2}^2+(\sigma_{i,1}^2-\sigma_{i,2}^2)o(1)]\norm{x}^2.
\]
By Lemma~\ref{air:t3:lem:singular} and $s_i=3(1+a i^{-2/3}+O(i^{-1}))$, division by $s_i^2$ leaves a strict loss of order $i^{-2/3}$. Output projection cannot increase the norm. This argument uses singular values of one channel and does not assume self-adjointness of $T_i$ or a frozen eigenvector equation for $\psi_i$.
\end{proof}

Let $q_i=|b_i|$ and $Z_i=\norm{z_i}$. The quantity $q_i+K i^{-1/3}Z_i$ is bounded for sufficiently large fixed $K$. Indeed the negative term $-Kci^{-1}Z_{i-1}$ absorbs the $O(i^{-1})Z_{i-1}$ cross term, while the remaining multiplicative errors are summable $O(i^{-4/3})$. Iterating the stable block gives
\[
Z_i=O(i^{-2/3}),\qquad b_i=b_\infty+O(i^{-1/3}).
\]
For the convolution estimate, sources older than $i/2$ have stretched-exponential suppression; the remaining kernel is bounded by
$\exp[-c(i-m)i^{-2/3}]$ and has total mass $O(i^{2/3})$.

Endpoint evaluation is bounded in the fixed norm. Since $\psi_i(0)\asymp i^{-1/2}$ at residue-zero times,
\[
\frac{z_i(0)}{\psi_i(0)}=O(i^{-1/6})\longrightarrow0.
\]
The scalar norm factors telescope, while
\[
\prod_{m=I}^i t_m=K_I 3^i e^{3a i^{1/3}}i^{5/2}
(1+O(i^{-1/3})),\qquad F_i(0)\sim\Ai'(a)i^{-1/3}.
\]
Consequently
\begin{equation}\label{air:t3:eq:dmain}
d_{3n}(0)\sim C_d27^n e^{\gamma n^{1/3}}n^{13/6}.
\end{equation}
The constant is finite. It is strictly positive because the published relaxed Theta lower bound in \cite{gdw} rules out a zero limit for this exact solution. Thus the proof does not assume positivity of a nonnormal spectral projection. Equation \eqref{air:t3:eq:factorial} and Stirling prove \eqref{air:t3:eq:Rmain}. The same argument applies to every fixed-time initial vector, giving a finite limiting endpoint ratio to the relaxed reference solution; that limit need not be known to be positive.

\section{Polynomial Airy recursion to every finite order}
Put $\tau=(1-\eps^3)^{-1/3}$ and seek formal series
\[
\Phi(\eps,x)=\sum_{m\ge0}\eps^m\phi_m(x),\qquad
\mathfrak s(\eps)=3+\sum_{m\ge2}\mathfrak s_m\eps^m,
\quad \phi_0=f,\quad \mathfrak s_2=3a.
\]
The exact formal evolving equation is
\begin{equation}\label{air:t3:eq:formal}
\mathcal U(\eps,x)\Phi(\eps\tau,(x-\eps)\tau)
+\Phi(\eps\tau,(x+2\eps)\tau)
=\mathfrak s(\eps)\Phi(\eps,x),
\end{equation}
where
\[
\mathcal U(\eps,x)=2-\frac{6(x\eps^2-3\eps^3)}{2+x\eps^2-\eps^3}.
\]
Require $\phi_m(0)=0$ at every order. At order $\eps^{m+2}$ the only new profile is $\phi_m$, and the only new scalar is $\mathfrak s_{m+2}$. Zero drift cancels the intervening order. After reducing derivatives by $f''=(x+a)f$, the equation is
\begin{equation}\label{air:t3:eq:recursion}
3(\partial_x^2-x-a)\phi_m
=\mathfrak s_{m+2}f+P_m f+Q_m f',
\end{equation}
with known polynomials $P_m,Q_m$.

For a polynomial--Airy function $Af+Bf'$,
\[
(\partial_x^2-x-a)(Af+Bf')
=[A''+2(x+a)B'+B]f+[2A'+B'']f'.
\]
After dividing \eqref{air:t3:eq:recursion} by three, polynomial forcing $Pf+Qf'$ is solved by
\begin{equation}\label{air:t3:eq:K}
\mathcal K B:=-\tfrac12B'''+2(x+a)B'+B=P-\tfrac12Q',
\qquad A'=\tfrac12(Q-B'').
\end{equation}
On polynomials of degree at most $d$, $\mathcal K$ has diagonal entries $1,3,\ldots,2d+1$ and lower-degree remaining terms. It is invertible. Adding a constant to the right side adds precisely that constant to $B$. The new scalar therefore uniquely enforces $B(0)=0$, which is the boundary condition $\phi_m(0)=0$. Fix $A(0)=0$ to remove the remaining amplitude gauge. This constructs every finite stage with no Fredholm obstruction or logarithmic profile.

For example, the first scalar coefficients in this gauge are
\[
\mathfrak s_3=\frac{15}{2},\quad
\mathfrak s_4=\frac{23a^2}{45},\quad
\mathfrak s_5=\frac{11a}{270},\quad
\mathfrak s_6=-\frac{1714a^3}{14175}-\frac{5293}{1890}.
\]
Two independent exact symbolic recursions verify these values. The infinite-stage existence statement follows from \eqref{air:t3:eq:K}, not from those finite computations.

\subsection{Uniform remainders and a common amplitude}
Fix $p>1$, take an integer $K\ge\max(4,\lceil3p\rceil)$, construct profiles through $K-2$ and scalars through $K$, and allow one further order if desired. Let
\[
G_i=3^i e^{3a i^{1/3}}i^{5/2},\qquad
H_i=G_i\exp\left(\sum_{r=1}^{K-3}h_r i^{-r/3}\right)
\]
with leading constant exactly one. Match $\log(H_i/H_{i-1})$ to $\log\mathfrak s(i^{-1/3})$. The first two nonconstant orders already agree. The new $h_r$ first appears at order $\eps^{r+3}$ with coefficient $-r/3$, so every further scalar equation is uniquely soluble.

Apply the same cutoff to the finite profile sum $\Phi_i$. Each fixed derivative of each polynomial--Airy correction has a bound
$C(1+x)^D e^{-c x^{3/2}}$ for $x\ge1$ and is bounded near zero. On $x\le2\log i$, the rational denominators in \eqref{air:t3:eq:formal} stay uniformly away from zero. Taylor remainders are powers of $\eps$ times a common integrable polynomial--Airy envelope; spatial displacements are $O(\eps+\eps^3x)$. Thus weighted mesh sums divide by $N_i^2\asymp\eps^{-1}$ without logarithmic loss. Bottom values vanish exactly, the physical top lies beyond the cutoff, and cutoff discrepancies are superpolynomially small. These estimates prove
\begin{equation}\label{air:t3:eq:highres}
\norm{T_i[H_{i-1}\Phi_{i-1}]-H_i\Phi_i}_\omega
\le C_p H_iN_i i^{-p}.
\end{equation}
Constants and starting indices may depend on the requested finite order.

Fix the same lower index $I$ in every construction and write
\[
P_i=\prod_{m=I}^i t_m\sim\kappa G_i,\qquad
S_i=\prod_{m=I}^i s_m=P_i\frac{N_i}{N_{I-1}}.
\]
Then
\[
Z_i=\frac{\kappa}{N_{I-1}}\frac{H_i\Phi_i}{S_i}
\]
has projection onto $\psi_i$ tending to one, independently of truncation. Indeed $H_i/G_i\to1$ and $\Phi_i/N_i-\psi_i\to0$. Dividing \eqref{air:t3:eq:highres} by $S_i$ yields
$\norm{Z_i-A_iZ_{i-1}}=O(i^{-p})$.
The reciprocal constants in this normalization are essential; one cannot independently absorb a different constant into each finite truncation.

\subsection{The zero-limit bootstrap}
Let the exact normalized solution have limiting projection $C$, and set
$E_i=y_i-CZ_i=\eta_i\psi_i+w_i$, where $\eta_i\to0$.
The already proved block estimates imply
\begin{align*}
|\eta_i-\eta_{i-1}|&\le
C i^{-4/3}|\eta_{i-1}|+C i^{-1}\norm{w_{i-1}}+C i^{-p},\\
\norm{w_i}&\le(1-ci^{-2/3})\norm{w_{i-1}}
+C i^{-4/3}|\eta_{i-1}|+C i^{-p}.
\end{align*}
If $\eta_i=O(i^{-q})$, the contraction convolution gives
\[
\norm{w_i}=O(i^{-q-2/3}+i^{2/3-p}).
\]
Summing the central increment \emph{backward}, using $\eta_\infty=0$ and $p>1$, gives
\[
\eta_i=O(i^{-q-1/3}+i^{1-p}).
\]
Starting at bounded $\eta$ and iterating finitely many times yields
$\norm{E_i}=O(i^{1-p})$. Endpoint extraction costs at most $i^{1/2}$, so the relative endpoint error is $O(i^{3/2-p})$. For the $M$-term theorem choose $p>3/2+(M+1)/3$ and sufficiently many formal terms. The sufficiently large starting index of these estimates may depend on $M$, while the scalar normalization retains its fixed lower limit $I$. This transfers every finite order with the same amplitude.

Finally, every $\phi_m$ vanishes at zero, so $\Phi_i(0)$ has an ordinary expansion in $\eps$ after factoring out $\Ai'(a)\eps$. Independent calculations give
\[
h_1=\frac{89a^2}{90},\qquad h_2=\frac{571a}{135},\qquad
\frac{\Phi_i(0)}{\Ai'(a)\eps}
=1+\frac{4a}{135}\eps^2-\frac{103}{810}\eps^3+O(\eps^4).
\]
Combining the scalar factor, endpoint series, $i=3n$, and Stirling proves Theorem~\ref{air:t3:thm:orders}, including its displayed coefficients.

\section{Positive defects and the automaton amplitude}\label{air:t3:sec:defects}
The ternary DFA recurrence is
\[
b_{x,m}=2b_{x,m-1}+(m+1)b_{x-1,m}-m b_{x-3,m-1},
\qquad x\ge2m.
\]
The base row is $b_{x,0}=1$ for $x\ge0$. The necessary auxiliary boundary value is $b_{-1,0}=1$; all other out-of-domain values vanish. Equivalently the first row is $b_{x,1}=2^{x-1}-1$ for $x\ge2$. This boundary convention reproduces the source's tabulated initial counts.

We use a positive completed-run representation rather than the signed recurrence. At level $m\ge1$, put
\[
V_m(\ell)=(m+1)^\ell,\qquad
W_m(\ell)=
\begin{cases}
2(m+1)^\ell,&0\le\ell<3,\\
2(m+1)^\ell-(m+1)^{\ell-2},&\ell\ge3.
\end{cases}
\]
Start both completed-run arrays at $a(x,1)=A(x,1)=1$ for $x\ge2$, with triangular support. Convolution by $V_m$ gives $a(x,m+1)$; convolution by $W_m$ gives $A(x,m+1)$. The endpoints are $R_n=a(2n,n)$ and $B_n=A(2n,n)$. For verification,
\[
\sum_{\ell\ge0}W_m(\ell)t^\ell
=\frac{2-(m+1)t^3}{1-(m+1)t},
\]
and appending the final horizontal run recovers the signed recurrence. In particular all weights in this representation are nonnegative.

Pointwise,
\[
2\left(1-\frac1{2(m+1)^2}\right)V_m(\ell)
\le W_m(\ell)\le2V_m(\ell).
\]
Induction gives
\begin{equation}\label{air:t3:eq:comparison}
\prod_{j=2}^n\left(1-\frac1{2j^2}\right)
\le\frac{B_n}{2^{n-1}R_n}\le1.
\end{equation}
Euler's sine product evaluates the infinite lower product as $P_3$ in Theorem~\ref{air:t3:thm:main}.
\begin{writenote}
This is the case $k=3$ of Theorem~\ref{air:ka:al:thm:main} (source 29), whose proof of the run weights goes through the completed-run generating series as here.
\end{writenote}


Under the relaxed bridge measure to $(2n,n)$, let $L_m$ be the horizontal-run length at vertical level $m$ before rising to $m+1$. Then
\begin{equation}\label{air:t3:eq:defects}
\rho_n:=\frac{B_n}{2^{n-1}R_n}
=\mathbb E_n\prod_{m=1}^{n-1}
\left(1-\frac{\one_{\{L_m\ge3\}}}{2(m+1)^2}\right).
\end{equation}
The factor $2^{n-1}$ is constant over the colored relaxed paths, so coloring does not change this bridge probability.

\subsection{A corrected uniform high-path bound}\label{air:t3:subsec:hightail}
\begin{writenote}
That the published lemma asserts $U_i(j)\le2$ is source 66's statement, not checked against the paper by this report (Part~0). The repair below, with the monotonicity of $u_r$ and the bridge comparison, is a second route to the automaton amplitude at $k=3$. Source 18 needs none of it: its signed-delay route (Section~\ref{air:ka:subsec:comparison} and the following subsections) uses only $U_i(j)\le q\alpha_i$.
\end{writenote}

The proof of the high-path lemma in \cite{gdw} (published Lemma~15, page~15:12; Lemma~14 in arXiv v1) contains the pointwise assertion $U_i(j)\le2$. That assertion is false near the boundary, for example $U_4(1)=8/3$. The following repair proves the required conclusion.

At active up destinations $j\ge1$,
\[
U_i(j)\le U_i(1)=2\left(1+\frac3{2i+1}\right)
\le2\left(1+\frac3{2i}\right).
\]
The product of the extra factors along any path of length $t$ is at most $Ct^{3/2}$. Thus
\begin{equation}\label{air:t3:eq:binomial}
d_{3x}(3y)\le Cx^{3/2}2^{2x+y}\binom{3x}{x-y}.
\end{equation}

For continuations to $(T,0)$, write $p(r,s;T)$ for their total weight and $u_r(s)=p(r,s;T)/(s+1)$. Its backward coefficients are
\[
A_r(s)=\frac{4(r-s+3)(s+2)}{(2r+s+3)(s+1)},\qquad
B_r(s)=\frac{(s-1)_+}{s+1}.
\]
For $s\ge1$ their row sum is
\[
V_r(s)=3-6\frac{s-1}{s+1}\frac{s+2}{2r+s+3},
\]
which decreases because both nonnegative fractions increase. At $s=0$ the sole coefficient is at least four, whereas $V_r(1)=3$, so the boundary comparison also holds. Adjacent same-phase starts $s,s+3$ have next supports $\{s-2,s+1\}$ and $\{s+1,s+4\}$. If the next normalized continuation decreases, all values on the first support are at least the common middle value and all on the second are at most it. The row-sum comparison preserves monotonicity. The terminal sequence is one at zero and zero at other physical sites, so reverse induction proves that $u_r$ decreases on each phase. Consequently
\[
p(3x,3y;T)\le(3y+1)p(3x,0;T),
\]
and comparison through height zero yields
\begin{equation}\label{air:t3:eq:bridge}
\mathbb P_T\{\text{path visits }(3x,3y)\}
\le(3y+1)\frac{d_{3x}(3y)}{d_{3x}(0)}.
\end{equation}

The relaxed lower bound gives
$d_{3x}(0)\ge c27^x e^{-Cx^{1/3}}x^{13/6}$.
Furthermore,
\[
\frac{2^{2x+y}}{27^x}\binom{3x}{x-y}
=\mathbb P\{\operatorname{Bin}(3x,1/3)=x-y\}
\le e^{-2y^2/(3x)}.
\]
The last inequality follows from the binomial lower-tail Chernoff bound. Combining \eqref{air:t3:eq:binomial} and \eqref{air:t3:eq:bridge}, summing over $y>x^{3/4}$ and $y\le x$, and then over $x>I$, bounds the forbidden bridge mass by
\begin{equation}\label{air:t3:eq:uniformtail}
\sum_{x>I} Cx^{4/3}
\exp\left(-\frac23\sqrt x+Cx^{1/3}\right)\longrightarrow0.
\end{equation}
This estimate is uniform in the terminal size. The correction to the false critical-weight domination is only polynomial and is harmless here.

\subsection{From fixed height to fixed time}\label{air:t3:subsec:fixedtime}
In \eqref{air:t3:eq:defects}, first retain only factors with $m<M$, obtaining $\rho_n^{[M]}$. Pointwise product comparison gives, uniformly in $n$,
\[
|\rho_n-\rho_n^{[M]}|\le\frac12\sum_{j>M}j^{-2}\le\frac1{2M}.
\]
For fixed $M$, additionally ignore a retained factor whose terminating rise occurs after path time $L$, obtaining $\rho_n^{[M,L]}$. This depends only on a fixed finite prefix. Summing the reweighted prefixes gives a finite initial vector at time $L$; afterward the transfer is exactly relaxed. The tracking theorem therefore proves convergence of $\rho_n^{[M,L]}$ as $n\to\infty$.

If a retained rise occurs at time $i>L$, then its level is $m+1\le M$ and its transformed height is
$j=i-3(m+1)\ge i-3M$.
At the preceding block time $p=3\lfloor i/3\rfloor$, the height is at least $p-3M$, because a forward step raises height by at most one. For fixed $M$ and sufficiently large $p$, this exceeds $3(p/3)^{3/4}$. The uniform tail bound \eqref{air:t3:eq:uniformtail} therefore gives
\[
\sup_n|\rho_n^{[M]}-\rho_n^{[M,L]}|\longrightarrow0
\qquad(L\to\infty).
\]
Thus $\rho_n^{[M]}$ converges as a uniform limit of convergent sequences; then the $1/(2M)$ estimate proves convergence of $\rho_n$. Its limit lies in $[P_3,1]$ by \eqref{air:t3:eq:comparison}. Combining with the relaxed equivalent proves the DFA part of Theorem~\ref{air:t3:thm:main}, including $C_B=\rho C_R/2$.

This transfer is qualitative in its truncation levels. It does not yield a finite-order DFA expansion.

\section{Inverse asymptotics and integer thresholds}\label{air:t3:sec:inverse}
\begin{writenote}
An instance of theorems \repopath{p0:thm:lambert-core}, \repopath{p0:thm:perturbed-inversion} and \repopath{p0:thm:staircase} of the repository's transseries volume (Part~0); no novelty is claimed for the inversion mechanics.
\end{writenote}

Let $T_n$ denote either $R_n$ or $B_n$, and set respectively
\[
(A,C)=(27/4,C_R)\quad\text{or}\quad(27/2,C_B).
\]
For $y\to\infty$, define
\[
L=\log y,\qquad w=W_0\left(\frac{\sqrt A\,L}{2e}\right),\qquad
n_0=\frac{L}{2w},\qquad D=2(w+1),
\]
where $W_0$ is the principal real Lambert branch. Let $x(y)$ be the inverse of the linear interpolation of $\log T_n$ between consecutive integers, with target value $\log y$.

\begin{corollary}[Inverse equivalent and corrections]\label{air:t3:cor:inverse}
For both counting sequences,
\begin{align}
x(y)&=n_0-\frac{\gamma n_0^{1/3}+(8/3)\log n_0+\log(2\pi C)}{D}
+o(1/\log n_0)\label{air:t3:eq:inverse1}\\
&=n_0-\frac{\gamma n_0^{1/3}}{D}-\frac43
-\frac{\log(2\pi C)-(4/3)\log A}{D}+o(1/\log n_0).\notag
\end{align}
The least integer threshold $\nu(y)=\min\{n:T_n\ge y\}$ equals $\lceil x(y)\rceil$ eventually. In particular \eqref{air:t3:eq:inverse1} gives its value with an $O(1)$ final error. Rounding the displayed approximation itself is not an exact uniform formula near integer thresholds.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
Stirling and the leading equivalent give
\[
\log T_n=H_0(n)+g(n)+o(1),
\]
where
\[
H_0(t)=2t\log t+(\log A-2)t,\qquad
g(t)=\gamma t^{1/3}+\frac83\log t+\log(2\pi C).
\]
The coefficient $8/3$ includes the logarithm from the two factorials. Consecutive log increments are $2\log n+\log A+o(1)>0$, so both inverses are eventually well defined. Linear interpolation of the smooth model adds $O(1/n)$, and interpolation of the sequence's $o(1)$ error remains uniformly $o(1)$.

The equation $H_0(n_0)=L$ has the stated exact Lambert solution, with $H_0'(n_0)=D$. Put $\delta=-g(n_0)/D=O(n_0^{1/3}/\log n_0)$. Taylor's formula gives
\[
H_0(n_0+\delta)+g(n_0+\delta)-L
=O(\delta^2/n_0)+O(n_0^{-2/3}|\delta|)=o(1).
\]
The actual interpolant has slopes asymptotic to $2\log n_0$, proving the error in \eqref{air:t3:eq:inverse1}. The second expression follows from $(8/3)\log n_0=(4/3)D-(4/3)\log A$. Exact rounding applies to the actual inverse by monotonicity, not automatically to its approximation.
\end{proof}

In particular $x(y)\sim L/(2\log L)$. Since $\gamma<0$, the stretched correction increases the inverse by
\[
-\frac{\gamma n_0^{1/3}}D
\sim\frac{-\gamma}{2^{4/3}}\frac{L^{1/3}}{(\log L)^{4/3}}.
\]

For relaxed trees, Theorem~\ref{air:t3:thm:orders} also gives constructive inverses to every finite order, provided the interpolation convention is retained. Taking logarithms and Stirling yields a smooth truncation
\[
H_M(t)=H_0(t)+g(t)+\sum_{r=1}^M\lambda_r t^{-r/3},
\]
with error $O(n^{-(M+1)/3})$ at integers. Let $\mathcal I$ denote linear interpolation and define $x_M$ by $(\mathcal I H_M)(x_M)=L$. Then
\[
x(y)-x_M=O\left(\frac{n_0^{-(M+1)/3}}{\log n_0}\right).
\]
This statement requires no derivative bounds on the enumeration remainder. A smooth-model inverse instead solves $H_M(z_M)=L$ and can be obtained by Newton iteration from $n_0$; its error squares with factor $O((n_0\log n_0)^{-1})$ at each step. These two inverses differ at high order. In fact, with $\theta=\{z_M\}$,
\[
x_M-z_M=-\frac{\theta(1-\theta)}{z_M H_M'(z_M)}
+O\left(\frac{z_M^{-5/3}}{\log z_M}\right).
\]
The fractional-part term comes from the chord error of the convex leading logarithm. A pure smooth inverse series must not silently be identified with the actual log-linear inverse beyond this order. No analogous all-orders DFA conclusion is needed or asserted here.

\section{Verification and scope}\label{air:t3:sec:verification}
The accompanying reproducibility package contains two independent exact polynomial--Airy recursion implementations, rational checks of the critical and moving boundary identities, exact completed-run and backward-monotonicity checks, and diagnostic numerical residual and inverse tests. The diagnostic floating checks verify implementations and signs; the proofs above supply the infinite-range conclusions.

Independent reviews cover the singular compactness and quasimode argument, the every-fixed-order transfer, the DFA amplitude transfer with the repaired tail, and the inverse conventions. The report keeps the inherited relaxed Theta theorem separate from its new proof steps. It includes no full third-party paper and makes no claim of an exhaustive novelty search. All results here are for the ternary case. The leading amplitudes are positive but are not numerically or algebraically evaluated.

\addtocontents{toc}{\protect\smallskip\protect\noindent\textit{Orders of growth by the completed-run comparison (source 29)}\protect\par}
\bigskip\noindent{\Large\bfseries Orders of growth by the completed-run comparison (source 29)}\par\nopagebreak
\begin{partsource}
Batch-77 manuscript 29, archive \repopath{larger-alphabet-automata-report.zip}, main file \repopath{article/larger-alphabet-automata.tex} (344 lines; 6-page PDF), titled ``Finite languages over larger alphabets: Summable exclusion and sharp order estimates'', dated 2 October 2026, empty author line. Handed over by cluster P4 and printed in full as the comparison section of Part~\ref{air:part:ka}, label prefix \texttt{air:ka:al:}.
\end{partsource}

\paragraph{Abstract of source 29.}
\begin{quote}\small
For each fixed alphabet size $k\ge3$, we transfer the known sharp order of
relaxed $k$-ary trees to minimal deterministic automata recognizing finite
languages. The proof is a positive completed-run representation: exclusion
reduces each run weight by at most $1/[2(m+1)^{k-1}]$, whose sum converges.
We obtain an explicit uniform comparison constant, the factorial,
exponential, Airy and polynomial scales, a uniform finite-level approximation,
and a logarithmic inverse enclosure. We do not prove a limiting amplitude
or an all-orders expansion. A boundary calculation explains why the binary
one-step singular-profile argument does not extend without modification.
\end{quote}


\begin{writenote}
Source 29 is the earliest form of the positive completed-run comparison that sources 18 and 66 use as a proof step (\eqref{air:ka:eq:products}, \eqref{air:t3:eq:comparison}); Theorem~\ref{air:ka:al:thm:main} is where this report states it. Its $\Theta$ conclusion \eqref{air:ka:al:eq:theta} is superseded by Theorem~\ref{air:ka:thm:all}, which proves a positive amplitude and every fixed order. What remains source 29's own: the explicit finite-$n$ constant $P_{k,n}$, uniform in $k\ge3$ and $n\ge1$; the explicit lower bound $P_k\ge(2\sqrt2/\pi)\sin(\pi/\sqrt2)$; the uniform finite-level approximation of Proposition~\ref{air:ka:al:prop:trunc}; the $\Theta$-only inverse enclosure of Theorem~\ref{air:ka:al:thm:inverse}, with the injection $B_{n+1}\ge kB_n$; the count by total states; and the boundary obstruction of Section~\ref{air:ka:al:sec:open}, which the ternary weight (Lemma~\ref{air:t3:lem:harmonic}) and Lemma~\ref{air:ka:lem:weight} resolve. Source 66's transfer audit lists source 29's research proof among its audited inputs.
\end{writenote}

\section{Statement and state convention}\label{air:ka:al:sec:statement}
Fix an integer $k\ge3$, and write $q=k-1$. Let $B_n$ count finite languages
over a fixed ordered $k$-letter alphabet whose minimal complete deterministic
automaton has exactly $n$ transient states and one rejecting sink, up to
state isomorphism. Thus $B_0=1$ counts the empty language, and $B_1=1$ counts
$\{\varepsilon\}$. Let $R_n$ count relaxed $k$-ary trees with $n$ internal
nodes, in the convention of Ghosh Dastidar--Wallner~\cite{gdw}.

Set
\begin{equation}\label{air:ka:al:eq:constants}
C_k=\frac{2k^k}{q^q},\qquad
\beta_k=3z\left(\frac{kq}{2}\right)^{1/3},\qquad
\alpha_k=\frac{2k-1}{3},
\end{equation}
where $z=-2.338107410\ldots$ is the largest negative zero of $\Ai$.
\begin{theorem}\label{air:ka:al:thm:main}
For every $n\ge1$,
\begin{equation}\label{air:ka:al:eq:comparison}
P_{k,n}\le\frac{B_n}{2^{n-1}R_n}\le1,\qquad
P_{k,n}=\prod_{j=2}^{n}\left(1-\frac1{2j^{k-1}}\right).
\end{equation}
Furthermore,
\begin{equation}\label{air:ka:al:eq:positive-product}
P_k:=\prod_{j=2}^{\infty}\left(1-\frac1{2j^{k-1}}\right)
\ge \frac{2\sqrt2}{\pi}\sin\frac{\pi}{\sqrt2}>0.
\end{equation}
Consequently, for each fixed $k\ge3$,
\begin{equation}\label{air:ka:al:eq:theta}
B_n=\Theta\!\left((n!)^{k-1}C_k^n
\exp(\beta_kn^{1/3})n^{\alpha_k}\right).
\end{equation}
\end{theorem}
The only asymptotic input is the published relaxed-tree theorem~\cite[Theorem~1]{gdw}.
The comparison~\eqref{air:ka:al:eq:comparison} is exact and elementary. Its lower
constant is uniform in both $k\ge3$ and $n\ge1$; the unspecified constants
in~\eqref{air:ka:al:eq:theta} may depend on $k$.
For ternary alphabets the scales are
\[
(n!)^2(27/2)^n\exp(3\cdot3^{1/3}zn^{1/3})n^{5/3}.
\]

\begin{remark}[Total states]
The exponent $\alpha_k=(2k-1)/3$ uses \emph{transient} states.
If $N=n+1$ is the total number of states and $D_N=B_{N-1}$, then
\[
D_N=\Theta\!\left((N!)^{k-1}C_k^N
\exp(\beta_kN^{1/3})N^{(2-k)/3}\right).
\]
Indeed $(N-1)!=N!/N$, and the remaining shifted factors change only by a
bounded factor tending to a positive constant.
\end{remark}

\section{Exact recurrence and positive completed runs}\label{air:ka:al:sec:runs}
The arrays in~\cite[Propositions~8 and~12]{gdw} satisfy, on $x\ge qm\ge q$,
\begin{align}
r_{x,m}&=r_{x,m-1}+(m+1)r_{x-1,m},\label{air:ka:al:eq:r}\\
b_{x,m}&=2b_{x,m-1}+(m+1)b_{x-1,m}-m b_{x-k,m-1}.\label{air:ka:al:eq:b}
\end{align}
Their base row is $r_{x,0}=b_{x,0}=1$ for $x\ge0$, and they vanish when
$x<qm$ for $m\ge1$. Retain the auxiliary value $b_{-1,0}=1$; all other
negative-coordinate values are zero. Then
\[
R_n=r_{qn,n},\qquad B_n=b_{qn,n}.
\]
The auxiliary boundary is essential: omitting it would give $B_1=2$, in
conflict with the combinatorial interpretation and Table~3 of~\cite{gdw}.
Equivalently, prescribe
$b_{x,1}=2^{x-k+2}-1$ for $x\ge k-1$ and use~\eqref{air:ka:al:eq:b} above that row.
This makes explicit the first-state convention implicit in the terse
published recurrence and consistent with the binary case~\cite{dfa}.
\begin{writenote}
The binary recurrence with this auxiliary value is Part~\ref{air:part:dfa}'s \eqref{air:dfa:eq:b-count}.
\end{writenote}


For $m\ge1$ and $\ell\ge0$ define
\begin{equation}\label{air:ka:al:eq:weights}
V_m(\ell)=(m+1)^\ell,\qquad
W_m(\ell)=2(m+1)^\ell-
\mathbf1_{\{\ell\ge k\}}(m+1)^{\ell-k+1}.
\end{equation}
Let $a(x,1)=A(x,1)=1$ for $x\ge q$. Both arrays vanish for $x<qm$.
For $x\ge q(m+1)$ set
\begin{align}
a(x,m+1)&=\sum_{\ell=0}^{x-qm}V_m(\ell)a(x-\ell,m),\label{air:ka:al:eq:renewal-r}\\
A(x,m+1)&=\sum_{\ell=0}^{x-qm}W_m(\ell)A(x-\ell,m).\label{air:ka:al:eq:renewal-b}
\end{align}
\begin{proposition}\label{air:ka:al:prop:renewal}
All these weights are nonnegative. For $x\ge qm$ and $m\ge1$,
\begin{align}
r_{x,m}&=\sum_{\ell=0}^{x-qm}(m+1)^\ell a(x-\ell,m),\label{air:ka:al:eq:recover-r}\\
b_{x,m}&=\sum_{\ell=0}^{x-qm}(m+1)^\ell A(x-\ell,m).\label{air:ka:al:eq:recover-b}
\end{align}
In particular $R_n=a(qn,n)$ and $B_n=A(qn,n)$ for $n\ge1$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
The completed-run generating series is
\[
\sum_{\ell\ge0}W_m(\ell)t^\ell
=\frac{2-(m+1)t^k}{1-(m+1)t}.
\]
Thus~\eqref{air:ka:al:eq:renewal-b} and~\eqref{air:ka:al:eq:recover-b} give
$A(x,m+1)=2b_{x,m}-(m+1)b_{x-k,m}$, while adding the trailing
horizontal steps gives
$b_{x,m+1}=A(x,m+1)+(m+2)b_{x-1,m+1}$.
Their combination is~\eqref{air:ka:al:eq:b}. The first row in~\eqref{air:ka:al:eq:recover-b}
is $\sum_{\ell=0}^{x-q}2^\ell=2^{x-k+2}-1$.
Uniqueness of the triangular recurrence proves the identity.
Replacing the numerator by $1$ gives~\eqref{air:ka:al:eq:r} and~\eqref{air:ka:al:eq:recover-r}.
At $x=qn,m=n$ only the term $\ell=0$ survives.
\end{proof}

\section{Summable exclusion and its consequences}
\begin{proof}[Proof of Theorem~\ref{air:ka:al:thm:main}]
For every $m\ge1$ and $\ell\ge0$,
\[
2\left(1-\frac1{2(m+1)^{k-1}}\right)V_m(\ell)
\le W_m(\ell)\le2V_m(\ell).
\]
Induction in the nonnegative convolutions~\eqref{air:ka:al:eq:renewal-r}--\eqref{air:ka:al:eq:renewal-b}
therefore proves
\[
2^{m-1}P_{k,m}a(x,m)\le A(x,m)\le2^{m-1}a(x,m).
\]
The common trailing-run convolution preserves these inequalities for
$r,b$. Taking the endpoint proves~\eqref{air:ka:al:eq:comparison}.
Since $k\ge3$, the sum of the deficits $1/(2j^{k-1})$ converges and all
factors are positive; hence $P_k>0$. The factors increase with $k$.
Euler's sine product gives
\[
P_3=\frac{\prod_{j=1}^{\infty}(1-1/(2j^2))}{1-1/2}
=\frac{2\sqrt2}{\pi}\sin\frac{\pi}{\sqrt2}.
\]
Finally, the published theorem~\cite{gdw} is
\[
R_n=\Theta\!\left((n!)^{k-1}
\left(\frac{k^k}{(k-1)^{k-1}}\right)^n
\exp(\beta_kn^{1/3})n^{(2k-1)/3}\right).
\]
Combining it with~\eqref{air:ka:al:eq:comparison} proves~\eqref{air:ka:al:eq:theta}.
\end{proof}

The same comparison holds when $k=2$, but its lower product then tends to
zero like $n^{-1/2}$. It cannot recover the binary-DFA power $7/8$.
The summability threshold at $k=3$ is the reason this larger-alphabet
leading-order extension is simpler than the binary amplitude problem.
\begin{writenote}
The binary amplitude problem is solved in Part~\ref{air:part:dfa} (source 48): $b_n\sim\gamma n!8^ne^{3zn^{1/3}}n^{7/8}$ with $\gamma>0$, by cancelling a non-summable drift rather than by a summable comparison.
\end{writenote}


\begin{proposition}[Uniform finite-level approximation]\label{air:ka:al:prop:trunc}
For an integer $M\ge1$, retain $W_m$ at levels $m<M$, replace it by $2V_m$
for $m\ge M$, and keep the first row unchanged. Write $B_n^{[M]}$ for
the resulting endpoint count. For every $n\ge1$ and $k\ge3$,
\begin{equation}\label{air:ka:al:eq:trunc}
0\le1-\frac{B_n}{B_n^{[M]}}
\le\frac12\sum_{j>M}j^{-(k-1)}
\le\frac{1}{2(k-2)M^{k-2}}.
\end{equation}
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
The exact ratio lies between $1$ and
$\prod_{j=M+1}^{\max(M,n)}(1-1/(2j^{k-1}))$, by the same convolution
comparison. Use $1-\prod_j(1-u_j)\le\sum_j u_j$ and the integral bound
for the decreasing tail.
\end{proof}
If $B_n^{[M]}/(2^{n-1}R_n)$ converges for every fixed $M$,
then~\eqref{air:ka:al:eq:trunc} proves convergence of $B_n/(2^{n-1}R_n)$ by a uniform
approximation argument. This reduces, but does not solve, the ratio-limit problem.
\begin{writenote}
The ratio-limit problem is solved in Part~\ref{air:part:ka}: for $k=3$ by exactly this route in Section~\ref{air:t3:subsec:fixedtime} (source 66), and for every $k\ge3$, with the first defect and eventual monotonicity, by Theorem~\ref{air:ka:thm:ratio} (source 18).
\end{writenote}


\section{A logarithmic inverse enclosure}\label{air:ka:al:sec:inverse}
\begin{writenote}
An instance of theorems \repopath{p0:thm:lambert-core} and \repopath{p0:thm:staircase} of the repository's transseries volume (Part~0); no novelty is claimed for the inversion mechanics. Theorem~\ref{air:ka:thm:all} now gives the amplitude term that this enclosure leaves open, and Section~\ref{air:ka:sec:inverse} the corresponding finer inverse.
\end{writenote}

This section uses only~\eqref{air:ka:al:eq:theta}; it does not posit an unknown amplitude.
Put
\[
\lambda_k=C_k^{1/q}/\ee,\qquad
\eta_k=\alpha_k+q/2=\frac{7k-5}{6},
\]
and define for large positive $t$
\begin{equation}\label{air:ka:al:eq:F}
F_k(t)=q t\log(\lambda_kt)+\beta_kt^{1/3}+\eta_k\log t.
\end{equation}
Stirling's formula and~\eqref{air:ka:al:eq:theta} give the precise bounded-error statement
\begin{equation}\label{air:ka:al:eq:log}
\log B_n=F_k(n)+O_k(1).
\end{equation}
In particular the coefficient of $\log n$ is $\eta_k$, not $\alpha_k$.

For $y\to\infty$, let $W$ be the positive real Lambert function and set
\begin{align}
t&=\frac{y}{qW(\lambda_ky/q)},&
L&=1+\log(\lambda_kt),\label{air:ka:al:eq:t}\\
\nu_k(y)&=t-\frac{\beta_kt^{1/3}+\eta_k\log t}{qL}.\label{air:ka:al:eq:nu}
\end{align}
\begin{theorem}\label{air:ka:al:thm:inverse}
There are constants $K_k,y_k>0$ such that, for $y\ge y_k$,
\begin{equation}\label{air:ka:al:eq:inverse}
\left\lceil\nu_k(y)-\frac{K_k}{\log\nu_k(y)}\right\rceil
\le I_k(\ee^y)\le
\left\lceil\nu_k(y)+\frac{K_k}{\log\nu_k(y)}\right\rceil,
\end{equation}
where $I_k(X)=\min\{n\ge1:B_n\ge X\}$. Moreover
\[
n=\nu_k(\log B_n)+O_k(1/\log n).
\]
The constants here are existence bounds, not effective numerical certificates.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Write $f(t)=qt\log(\lambda_kt)$ and
$g(t)=\beta_kt^{1/3}+\eta_k\log t$. Formula~\eqref{air:ka:al:eq:t} gives $f(t)=y$
and $f'(t)=qL$. With $\delta=-g(t)/(qL)=O(t^{1/3}/\log t)$,
Taylor's formula yields
\[
F_k(t+\delta)-y
=O(\delta^2/t)+O((t^{-2/3}+t^{-1})|\delta|)=o(1).
\]
Since $F_k'(t)\sim q\log t>0$,~\eqref{air:ka:al:eq:log} implies the asserted
$O(1/\log n)$ inversion at sequence values.
Choose a constant bounding the error in~\eqref{air:ka:al:eq:log}. Increasing $K_k$
if necessary makes every sufficiently large integer below
$\nu_k(y)-K_k/\log\nu_k(y)$ have $\log B_n<y$, and every sufficiently
large integer at or above the upper endpoint have $\log B_n\ge y$.
Taking ceilings gives~\eqref{air:ka:al:eq:inverse}.

The threshold is well defined: for every nonempty finite language $L$, each
language $aL$, with $a$ a letter, has one more transient state in its minimal
complete automaton. The old initial residual $L$ is reached after $a$;
all its residuals remain reachable, and the new initial residual has strictly
larger maximum word length. The $k$ constructions are disjoint and injective,
so $B_{n+1}\ge kB_n$ for $n\ge1$.
\end{proof}
The enclosure respects integer rounding. It does not assert a smooth
interpolation canonically determined by the sequence, nor an everywhere
unambiguous rounded estimate without the displayed error interval.

\section{What remains open and a boundary obstruction}\label{air:ka:al:sec:open}
Neither the relaxed theorem nor the comparison proves that the quotient
in~\eqref{air:ka:al:eq:theta} has a limit. A positive limiting amplitude and
higher-order endpoint corrections for this family remain unproved here.
A relaxed amplitude theorem alone would still require control of the
finite-level perturbed ratios in Proposition~\ref{air:ka:al:prop:trunc}.
\begin{writenote}
A positive limiting amplitude, and the endpoint corrections to every fixed order, are proved for every fixed $k\ge3$ in Theorem~\ref{air:ka:thm:all} (source 18; for $k=3$ the amplitude also in Theorem~\ref{air:t3:thm:main}, source 66). The obstruction below is resolved there by the fixed weight $\omega=\ell/h$, which matches the forward and adjoint boundary harmonics on every residue phase (Lemmas~\ref{air:t3:lem:harmonic} and~\ref{air:ka:lem:weight}); the paragraph after Lemma~\ref{air:t3:lem:harmonic} names the residue-dependent extrapolation lengths computed here.
\end{writenote}


The critically tilted relaxed transfer has two shifts:
\[
(T_if)(j)=U(i,j)f(j-1)+f(j+k-1),\qquad
U(i,j)=\frac{(k-1)^2(i-j+k)}{(k-1)i+j}.
\]
For $k\ge3$, its directed zero pattern has a forward edge of length one
without its reverse. Positive diagonal conjugation cannot make it symmetric.
Although $T_i^*T_i$ is Jacobi on residue classes modulo $k$, the following
exact frozen calculation prevents an automatic binary singular-profile proof.

Let $T=q\,\mathrm{up}+\mathrm{down}_q$ on the half-line, and let $S$ be the
unilateral lower shift in residue coordinate $\ell\ge0$. From input residue
$r$ to output residue $r+1\pmod k$, the matrix is
\[
T_r=qI+S^*\quad(0\le r<q),\qquad T_q=I+qS.
\]
At the bulk squared edge $k^2$, a formal linear solution $\ell+c$ of the
right and left Jacobi boundary equations has constants
\[
\begin{array}{c|cc|c}
\text{input residue}&c_{\rm right}&c_{\rm left}&
\text{physical extrapolated zero}\\\hline
0\le r<q&q/k&1&r-q\\
r=q&1&1/k&-1
\end{array}
\]
For example the first right equation is
$q^2c+q(c+1)=k^2c$, giving $c=q/k$; the first left equation has bottom
diagonal $q^2+1$ and gives $c=1$. In physical coordinates the right and
left zeros agree within one step, but change between successive residues.
The boundary offsets cycle as $q,q-1,\ldots,1,1$, so only the binary case
has a constant offset. Thus an $O(i^{-1/3})$ boundary-scale mismatch may
occur between successive singular profiles, and an $O(i^{-1})$ tracking
estimate requires an additional cancellation argument. The finite operator's
Airy asymptotics are not asserted by this frozen calculation.

A whole $k$-step analysis or matched forward and adjoint boundary profiles
could address this issue. Also $U(i,j)$ can exceed $q$ near the boundary,
so global contraction requires its own normalization. These analytic
questions are separate from the positive proof of Theorem~\ref{air:ka:al:thm:main}.

\section{Verification and literature scope}\label{air:ka:al:sec:verification}
The attached standard-library Python verifier checks signed and positive
recurrences, all-cell comparisons, truncation bounds, and published terms
for $2\le k\le8$ through $16$ transient states. Independent small-automaton
checks and mathematical review are recorded separately. In particular,
\[
\begin{aligned}
k=3:&\quad 1,1,14,532,42644,6011320,1330452032,\ldots,\\
k=4:&\quad 1,1,30,3900,1460700,1220162880,\ldots.
\end{aligned}
\]
A331120 is the binary minimal-DFA sequence. A082162 counts relaxed ternary
trees, not minimal ternary DFAs. No larger-arity minimal-DFA OEIS identifier
was verified in the bounded search.

The 2024 primary paper's conclusion leaves compacted and minimal-DFA
asymptotics to a projected long version. The current author bibliography,
focused later-literature searches, and authorized ProveIt semantic searches
were checked on 2 October 2026; no matching later result was located.
Wallner's 2026 decompression paper~\cite{decomp} concerns a different statistic
on chains. These are bounded overlap checks, not a universal novelty guarantee.
The attached receipts specify the queries and limitations.
\begin{writenote}
The repository searches were right when made (the receipt refers to commit \texttt{4b874cea0}). The repository's material on these automata is now this report, which the same batch delivered. The raw search receipt \repopath{sources/overlap.json} is not shipped; see the README.
\end{writenote}


\part{Binary finite-language automata}\label{air:part:dfa}

\begin{partsource}
Batch-77 manuscript 48, archive \repopath{oeis-dfa-finite-language-report.zip}, main file \repopath{article/dfa-finite-languages.tex} (967 lines; 16-page PDF), titled ``Positive amplitude and all orders for finite binary languages'', dated 2 October 2026, empty author line, placed by commit \texttt{aa7345800} (cluster P2). Label prefix \texttt{air:dfa:}. Its passages that are word for word Part~\ref{air:part:cb}'s are replaced by pointers, and its appendix is Appendix~\ref{air:app:jacobi}; every passage in which the automaton differs is printed.
\end{partsource}

\paragraph{Abstract of the source.}
\begin{quote}\small
A331120 counts finite binary languages by the number of transient states in
their minimal deterministic automata. The known estimate has order
$n!8^n\exp(3zn^{1/3})n^{7/8}$, where $z$ is the largest negative Airy zero.
We strengthen it to a positive limiting amplitude and a relative expansion
at every finite order in $n^{-1/3}$. An exact nonnegative renewal model
establishes positivity and domination, while a half-strength signed delay
of a contractive Jacobi evolution identifies the polynomial correction.
A finite-memory projection argument and an arbitrary-order residual
bootstrap yield endpoint accuracy without assuming that the signed
propagator is positive. We give three explicit logarithmic corrections,
a constructive coefficient recursion, a selected smooth inverse, and the
proper distinction between inverse interpolation and discrete thresholds.
The shared spectral estimates are proved in a self-contained appendix.
The amplitude exists and is positive; its decimal digits are not certified.
\end{quote}


\section{Statement and mathematical scope}\label{air:dfa:sec:statement}
Let $b_{n,m}$ be the array
\begin{equation}\label{air:dfa:eq:b-count}
\begin{aligned}
b_{n,m}&=2b_{n,m-1}+(m+1)b_{n-1,m}-m b_{n-2,m-1}
&& (n\ge m\ge1),\\
b_{n,0}&=1 &&(n\ge-1),\qquad b_{n,m}=0\quad(m>n).
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}
The auxiliary value $b_{-1,0}=1$ is retained. Put $b_n=b_{n,n}$.
This is A331120, beginning
\[
1,1,6,60,900,18480,487560,15824880,612504240,\ldots.
\]
It counts minimal complete deterministic automata over a fixed binary
alphabet, up to isomorphism of states, with $n$ transient states and one
rejecting sink. Equivalently it counts the corresponding finite languages
by minimal-state complexity. Proposition~5 of \cite{dfa} proves the exact
recurrence. The same paper's Theorem~1 proves
\begin{equation}\label{air:dfa:eq:known-theta}
b_n=\Theta\!\left(n!8^n\ee^{3zn^{1/3}}n^{7/8}\right),
\end{equation}
where $z=-2.338107410459767\ldots$ is the largest real zero of $\Ai$.
The Airy exponential and exponent $7/8$ are therefore established inputs.
The additional result here is a convergent positive amplitude and its
complete finite algebraic correction hierarchy.

Set $a=2^{-1/3}z$ and $F(x)=\Ai(z+2^{1/3}x)$, so
$F''=2(x+a)F$, $F(0)=0$, and $F'(0)>0$.
\begin{theorem}\label{air:dfa:thm:main}
There is a constant $\gamma>0$ such that, for every fixed integer $M\ge0$,
\begin{equation}\label{air:dfa:eq:main}
b_n=\gamma n!8^n\ee^{3zn^{1/3}}n^{7/8}
\left(1+\sum_{k=1}^{M}\mathfrak c_k n^{-k/3}
+O_M(n^{-(M+1)/3})\right).
\end{equation}
All $\mathfrak c_k$ are constructible elements of $\Q[z]$. In logarithmic
form the first three corrections are
\begin{equation}\label{air:dfa:eq:log-corrections}
\begin{aligned}
\log\frac{b_n}{\gamma n!8^n\ee^{3zn^{1/3}}n^{7/8}}
={}&\frac{53z^2}{90}n^{-1/3}+\frac{623z}{432}n^{-2/3}\\
&+\left(\frac{3497}{4480}-\frac{1304z^3}{42525}\right)n^{-1}
+O(n^{-4/3}).
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}
\end{theorem}
This is a Poincar\'e expansion to every fixed finite order. It is not a
claim of an exponentially improved transseries or a canonical analytic
continuation of an integer sequence. Strict positivity of $\gamma$ uses
the published lower bound in \eqref{air:dfa:eq:known-theta} only after the
convergence and all-orders error estimates have been proved.
\begin{writenotejp}
Part~\ref{air:part:jp} (Report~103) proves this theorem a second time, in even time, taking positivity from the comparison $b_n\ge2^{n-1}c_n$ and the compacted lower bound only. It continues \eqref{air:dfa:eq:log-corrections} by $d_4=-5429z^2/19440$, \eqref{air:jp:dfa:eq:logcoeff}, gives $\mathfrak c_4$ ($c_4$ in \eqref{air:jp:dfa:eq:coefficients}) and the normalized ratio $b_n/(8nb_{n-1})$ through $n^{-7/3}$, \eqref{air:jp:dfa:eq:ratio}.
\end{writenotejp}

\section{Exact positivity and the initial boundary}\label{air:dfa:sec:positivity}
Normalize uniformly by $c_{n,m}=b_{n,m}/2^m$. Then
\begin{equation}\label{air:dfa:eq:count}
c_{n,m}=c_{n,m-1}+(m+1)c_{n-1,m}-\frac m2 c_{n-2,m-1}.
\end{equation}
In particular $c_{n,0}=1$, $c_{-1,0}=1$, and
$c_{n,1}=(2^n-1)/2$. A normalization by $2^{m-1}$ only for $m\ge1$
requires a separate boundary row; \eqref{air:dfa:eq:count} avoids that exception.
\begin{writenotejp}
Report~103 in Part~\ref{air:part:jp} uses the same uniform normalization, $B(n,m)=b(n,m)/2^m$ (this Part's $c_{n,m}$), and goes further: it states that the transformed arrays printed on pages 11:6--11:7 of \cite{dfa}, normalized by $2^{m-1}$ for $m\ge1$, give $\frac54$ instead of $\frac32$ at one entry when read uniformly (Section~\ref{air:jp:dfa:sec:exact}). That statement about the printed paper has not been checked here (Question~\ref{air:jp:q:normalization}); Report~103 adds that it is not a claim that the published theorem is false.
\end{writenotejp}

\begin{proposition}[Positive renewal representation]\label{air:dfa:prop:renewal}
Set $A(n,1)=1$ for every $n\ge1$ and $A(n,m)=0$ for $n<m$. For $m\ge1$
put
\begin{equation}\label{air:dfa:eq:run-weights}
W_m(k)=\begin{cases}
2,&k=0,\\
2(m+1),&k=1,\\
\bigl(2(m+1)^2-(m+1)\bigr)(m+1)^{k-2},&k\ge2.
\end{cases}
\end{equation}
For $n\ge m+1$, define
\[
A(n,m+1)=\sum_{k=0}^{n-m}W_m(k)A(n-k,m).
\]
Then
\begin{equation}\label{air:dfa:eq:renewal-recover}
b_{n,m}=\sum_{k=0}^{n-m}(m+1)^kA(n-k,m)\quad(m\ge1).
\end{equation}
All weights are nonnegative, and $b_n=A(n,n)$ for $n\ge1$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
The completed-run generating series is
\[
\sum_{k\ge0}W_m(k)t^k=\frac{2-(m+1)t^2}{1-(m+1)t}.
\]
Consequently completing a run followed by a vertical step gives
$A(n,m)=2b_{n,m-1}-m b_{n-2,m-1}$ for $m\ge2$.
Adding possible trailing horizontal steps in \eqref{air:dfa:eq:renewal-recover}
recovers \eqref{air:dfa:eq:b-count}. For $m=1$, the initial renewal values give
$b_{n,1}=\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}2^k=2^n-1$, which agrees with
\eqref{air:dfa:eq:b-count}, including its initial value $b_{-1,0}$.
The triangular cutoff ensures that only $k=0$ survives on $n=m$.
Uniqueness of the triangular recurrence proves the assertion.
\end{proof}
Combinatorially $A$ counts prefixes ending in a vertical step. At height
$m$, a horizontal run has $m+1$ choices per edge and two vertical colors;
the subtracted completed-run decorations are exactly the forbidden repeated
transition signature. The exceptional first row includes the mandatory
initial horizontal edge and the requirement that the first non-sink state
be accepting. The algebraic proof above makes the initial convention exact.

Let $r_{n,m}$ denote the relaxed array
$r_{n,0}=1$, $r_{n,m}=r_{n,m-1}+(m+1)r_{n-1,m}$, with invalid entries zero.
Since $c_{n,m}\ge0$, induction in \eqref{air:dfa:eq:count} yields
\begin{equation}\label{air:dfa:eq:domination}
0\le c_{n,m}\le r_{n,m}.
\end{equation}
This is a domination of exact counts; no positivity of the signed linear
propagator will be assumed.

\section{The shared Jacobi evolution}\label{air:dfa:sec:jacobi}

\begin{writenote}
Sections~\ref{air:dfa:sec:jacobi}--\ref{air:dfa:sec:bootstrap} and Appendix~A of source 48 follow the compacted-tree argument of Part~\ref{air:part:cb} line by line; only the renewal model, the delay coefficient (with its $m=1$ term), the drift $1/(8N)$, the seeds and the coefficients differ. Source 48 does not say so. Its Section~3 up to the paragraph below is, word for word, the beginning of Part~\ref{air:part:cb}'s Section~\ref{air:cb:sec:jacobi}: the gauge \eqref{air:cb:eq:gauge}, the Jacobi matrix $S_N$ with edge weights $b_{N,j}$ and the contraction $Q_N$ \eqref{air:cb:eq:S-Q}, the vectors $g_N,h_N$ and the operators $P_N,M_N,\Pi_N$ \eqref{air:cb:eq:ground-defs}, Lemma~\ref{air:cb:lem:jacobi} with \eqref{air:cb:eq:jacobi-estimates} and the finite profile \eqref{air:cb:eq:finite-profile}, and the remark that the gap is parity-restricted. It is not reprinted, and the references below to these displays and to Lemma~\ref{air:cb:lem:jacobi} are to Part~\ref{air:part:cb}. Source 48 adds only the conventions $D_0(0)=1$, $P_0=1$ and $v_0^{(r)}=\delta_0$. Its appendix ``Self-contained Jacobi estimates'' is Appendix~\ref{air:app:jacobi} with ``DFA theorem'' in place of ``compacted theorem'', and is not reprinted either.
\end{writenote}
The relaxed counts $r_{n,m}$ satisfy
\[
r_{n,0}=1,\qquad r_{n,m}=r_{n,m-1}+(m+1)r_{n-1,m},
\]
with invalid cells zero. By \eqref{air:dfa:eq:domination}, $0\le c_{n,m}\le r_{n,m}$. The common
gauge turns the relaxed recurrence into $v_N^{(r)}=S_NQ_Nv_{N-1}^{(r)}$;
consequently $\norm{v_N^{(r)}/P_N}\le1$. Only this domination and the explicit
Jacobi estimates, not an automatic extension of a relaxed amplitude theorem,
will be used to prove Theorem~\ref{air:dfa:thm:main}.

\section{A globally bounded signed delay}\label{air:dfa:sec:delay}

Set
\[
e_{N,j}=\frac{c_{(N+j)/2,(N-j)/2}}{((N+j)/2)!},\qquad
v_N(j)=\frac{e_{N,j}}{D_N(j)}
\]
on $0\le j\le N$, $j\equiv N\pmod2$, and zero otherwise. For $N\ge3$, direct substitution in \eqref{air:dfa:eq:count} gives
\begin{equation}\label{air:dfa:eq:ungauged-delay}
e_{N,j}=\frac{N-j+2}{N+j}e_{N-1,j-1}+e_{N-1,j+1}
-\frac{N-j}{(N+j)(N+j-2)}e_{N-3,j-1}.
\end{equation}
The physical recurrence starts at $N=3$, using the exact initial vectors
\[
e_0(0)=1,\qquad e_1(1)=1,\qquad e_2(0)=e_2(2)=\tfrac12,
\]
with all other entries zero. At $N=2,j=0$ the fictitious $c_{-1,0}=1$
contributes to \eqref{air:dfa:eq:count}; omitting it would change the seed.
For $N\ge3$ the delay is used only on $1\le j\le N-2$ with the correct
parity. It is zero at $j=0$ and absent on $m=0$. It is \emph{nonzero}
at $m=1$, $j=N-2$, unlike the compacted-tree delay.
\begin{writenote}
In Part~\ref{air:part:cb} the delay coefficient $2(N-j-2)/((N+j)(N+j-2))$ vanishes at $j=N-2$ (Section~\ref{air:cb:sec:delay}); here it is $(N-j)/((N+j)(N+j-2))$, nonzero there.
\end{writenote}


For all sufficiently large $N$, the exact gauge recurrence is
\begin{equation}\label{air:dfa:eq:gauged-delay}
v_N=S_NQ_Nv_{N-1}-L_Nv_{N-3},\qquad
(L_Nf)(j)=\ell_{N,j}f(j-1),
\end{equation}
where on $1\le j\le N-2$
\begin{equation}\label{air:dfa:eq:ell}
\ell_{N,j}=\frac{N-j}{(N+j)(N+j-2)}
\frac{D_{N-3}(j-1)}{D_N(j)},
\end{equation}
and it is zero elsewhere. Cancellation of factorials gives
\begin{equation}\label{air:dfa:eq:gauge-ratio}
\left(\frac{D_{N-3}(j-1)}{D_N(j)}\right)^2
=\frac{(N-j+1)(N-j)(N+j)(N+j-1)(N+j-2)(N+j-3)}
{(N+1)N^2(N-1)^2(N-2)}.
\end{equation}
For $N\ge6$ this ratio is uniformly bounded on the entire physical support;
the rational prefactor in \eqref{air:dfa:eq:ell} is $O(N^{-1})$. Hence
\begin{equation}\label{air:dfa:eq:global-delay}
\norm{L_N}\le C N^{-1}.
\end{equation}
This is a global operator bound, not an estimate limited to the Airy window.

For $1\le j\le2N^{1/2}$ on the physical support, expanding \eqref{air:dfa:eq:ell}--\eqref{air:dfa:eq:gauge-ratio} gives
\[
\ell_{N,j}=\frac1N+O\!\left(\frac{j+1}{N^2}\right).
\]
Let $U$ be the unilateral upward shift: $(Uf)(0)=0$ and $(Uf)(j)=f(j-1)$.
The finite profile in Lemma~\ref{air:cb:lem:jacobi}, its Airy moments, and
\eqref{air:dfa:eq:global-delay} imply
\begin{equation}\label{air:dfa:eq:shift-delay}
\norm{L_Ng_{N-3}-\tfrac1N Ug_{N-3}}=O(N^{-5/3}),\qquad
\norm{Ug_{N-3}-g_N}=O(N^{-1/3}).
\end{equation}
For the first estimate the typical height is $O(N^{1/3})$, and the
$O(N^{-4/3})$ quasimode error is multiplied by $O(N^{-1})$. Cutoff tails
are exponentially small. The second estimate uses a one-site sample shift
and an $O(N^{-1})$ time change. The shifted parity is exactly the parity
occupied at time $N$.

\section{Cancellation of the scalar drift}\label{air:dfa:sec:drift}

Put
\begin{equation}\label{air:dfa:eq:renormalize}
R_N=P_NN^{-1/8},\qquad u_N=v_N/R_N,
\end{equation}
and begin the following recurrences at any fixed sufficiently large $N$.
Then
\begin{equation}\label{air:dfa:eq:normalized-delay}
u_N=\eta_NM_Nu_{N-1}-B_Nu_{N-3},\qquad
\eta_N=\left(\frac N{N-1}\right)^{1/8},\qquad
B_N=\frac{R_{N-3}}{R_N}L_N.
\end{equation}
Since $R_{N-3}/R_N=1/8+O(N^{-2/3})$, Lemma~\ref{air:cb:lem:jacobi} and
\eqref{air:dfa:eq:shift-delay} yield
\begin{equation}\label{air:dfa:eq:drift}
\begin{aligned}
\eta_N&=1+\frac1{8N}+O(N^{-2}),& \norm{B_N}&=O(N^{-1}),\\
\norm{B_Ng_{N-3}-\tfrac1{8N}g_N}&=O(N^{-4/3}),\\
b_N:=\ip{g_N}{B_Ng_{N-3}}&=\frac1{8N}+O(N^{-4/3}),\\
\eta_Nt_N-1-b_N&=O(N^{-4/3}).
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}
The last line cancels the nonsummable order-$N^{-1}$ drift. It explains the
additional factor $N^{-1/8}$ relative to the relaxed spectral normalization.

\begin{writenote}
Source 48 states here the finite-memory Lemma~\ref{air:cb:lem:memory}, with \eqref{air:cb:eq:memory1}, \eqref{air:cb:eq:memory2} and its proof, word for word as in Part~\ref{air:part:cb}; it is not reprinted, and the references to it below are to Part~\ref{air:part:cb}.
\end{writenote}

\section{Convergence of the scalar amplitude}

The positive gauge preserves $0\le v_N\le v_N^{(r)}$ entrywise. The relaxed
contraction therefore gives the initial bound
\begin{equation}\label{air:dfa:eq:initial-domination}
\norm{u_N}\le N^{1/8}.
\end{equation}
Decompose
\[
u_N=A_Ng_N+w_N,\qquad w_N\perp g_N.
\]
Applying Lemma~\ref{air:cb:lem:jacobi} to the transverse part of
\eqref{air:dfa:eq:normalized-delay}, and bounding the delayed whole vector using
\eqref{air:dfa:eq:initial-domination}, gives
\[
\norm{w_N}\le(1-c'N^{-2/3})\norm{w_{N-1}}+O(N^{-7/8}).
\]
The factor $\eta_N=1+O(N^{-1})$ is absorbed in the gap. Lemma~\ref{air:cb:lem:memory}
then gives
\begin{equation}\label{air:dfa:eq:first-transverse}
\norm{w_N}=O(N^{-5/24}).
\end{equation}
No cancellation or positivity of the delayed evolution is needed here.

Projecting the recurrence gives
\begin{equation}\label{air:dfa:eq:scalar-recurrence}
