Theorem~\ref{air:jp:rb:thm:allorders} is an all-order algebraic, or Poincar\'e, expansion in a single dominant Airy sector. It does not identify contributions smaller than every power. A full transseries would require additional sectors, their amplitudes, and corresponding remainder estimates; these have not been established here.

\section{Explicit diagonal coefficients and numerical checks}\label{air:jp:rb:sec:coefficients}

Formal integration of $\log s$ by \eqref{air:jp:rb:eq:carrieridentity} and endpoint evaluation $F(t,t)$ give the coefficients $b_j$ of Theorem~\ref{air:jp:rb:thm:allorders}. The first six relaxed log coefficients are
\begin{align}
 b_1&=\frac{53a^2}{90},& b_2&=\frac{44a}{27},\notag\\
 b_3&=\frac{171315-5216a^3}{170100},& b_4&=-\frac{422a^2}{1215},\notag\\
 b_5&=\frac{a(717281a^3-298772235)}{344452500},&
 b_6&=\frac{2250428a^3-20962395}{41334300}.\label{air:jp:rb:eq:brelaxed}
\end{align}
In particular, the multiplicative expansion starts with
\[
 1+\frac{53a^2}{90}n^{-1/3}
 +\left[\frac12\left(\frac{53a^2}{90}\right)^2+\frac{44a}{27}\right]n^{-2/3}
 +O(n^{-1}).
\]
The all-order theorem justifies termwise shifts to any fixed required depth: take a deeper forward expansion than the desired ratio error before dividing. Thus the normalized relaxed ratio has
\begin{align}
 \frac{r_n}{4nr_{n-1}}
 &=1+an^{-2/3}+n^{-1}+\frac{41a^2}{135}n^{-4/3}
       +\frac{20a}{81}n^{-5/3}\notag\\
 &\quad+\frac{176a^3-1215}{170100}n^{-2}
       -\frac{427a^2}{3645}n^{-7/3}\notag\\
 &\quad-\frac{a(1035598a^3+3591495)}{103335750}n^{-8/3}
       +\frac{7774a^3}{295245}n^{-3}
       +O(n^{-10/3}).\label{air:jp:rb:eq:ratiorelaxed}
\end{align}
The coefficients through the familiar leading terms agree with the formal analysis in \cite[Section 3.4]{epfw}. The further displayed exact values are reproducible derivations relative to the inspected sources, not a claim that no one has computed them previously.
\begin{writenotejp}
With $a=z$, the coefficients $b_1,b_2,b_3$ are Part~\ref{air:part:rb}'s (F2) ($171315/170100=141/140$ and $5216/170100=1304/42525$). The coefficients $b_4,b_5,b_6$ and the ratio expansion \eqref{air:jp:rb:eq:ratiorelaxed} are new to this report: Part~\ref{air:part:rb} prints three logarithmic corrections and no ratio expansion. The conversion from \eqref{air:jp:rb:eq:brelaxed} to \eqref{air:jp:rb:eq:ratiorelaxed}, and from $b_1,b_2,b_3$ to Part~\ref{air:part:rb}'s (F3), was recomputed symbolically at the write (difference zero). Through $n^{-3}$ the ratio involves only $a$ and $b_1,\ldots,b_6$, because $b_j$ first enters at $n^{-j/3-1}$; its $O(n^{-10/3})$ remainder uses Theorem~\ref{air:jp:rb:thm:allorders} at depth $J=9$.
\end{writenotejp}

\subsection{Compacted formal comparison}
For compacted trees the formal diagonal exponent is $\alpha=3/4$. If one writes
\[
 \log\frac{c_n}{n!4^ne^{3an^{1/3}}n^{3/4}}
 \ \widehat{=}\ \log\gamma_c+\sum_{j\ge1}b_j^{(c)}n^{-j/3},
\]
the hat explicitly denotes a formal ansatz, not an asymptotic theorem for the actual counts. The same algebra gives
\begin{align}
 b_1^{(c)}&=\frac{53a^2}{90},&b_2^{(c)}&=\frac{271a}{216},\notag\\
 b_3^{(c)}&=\frac{477495-41728a^3}{1360800},&
 b_4^{(c)}&=-\frac{2053a^2}{9720},\notag\\
 b_5^{(c)}&=\frac{a(2869124a^3-329492565)}{1377810000},&
 b_6^{(c)}&=-\frac{320744a^3+10924065}{132269760}.\label{air:jp:rb:eq:bcompacted}
\end{align}
The formal ratio is
\begin{align}
 \frac{c_n}{4nc_{n-1}}
 &\ \widehat{=}\ 1+an^{-2/3}+\tfrac34n^{-1}
       +\frac{41a^2}{135}n^{-4/3}+\frac{20a}{81}n^{-5/3}\notag\\
 &\quad+\frac{704a^3+207765}{680400}n^{-2}
       -\frac{454a^2}{3645}n^{-7/3}\notag\\
 &\quad-\frac{a(8284784a^3+153117585)}{826686000}n^{-8/3}
       +\frac{11327392a^3+50486895}{330674400}n^{-3}+\cdots.
\end{align}
The common $n^{-5/3}$ coefficient is not a copying error: it equals
$a(\alpha+1/3)-(2/3)b_2$, and the changes in $\alpha$ and $b_2$ cancel. No convergence of the compacted normalization or analytic remainder for these coefficients follows from the formal recursion alone.
\begin{writenotejp}
The analytic input that this subsection lacks now exists twice: Report~101 (Theorem~\ref{air:jp:cb:thm:allorders}, with the same six coefficients in \eqref{air:jp:cb:eq:logexpansion}) and Part~\ref{air:part:cb} (Theorem~\ref{air:cb:thm:main}, whose (F2)-type corrections are $b^{(c)}_1,b^{(c)}_2,b^{(c)}_3$). By Corollary~\ref{air:jp:cor:compacted} both hatted expansions above hold as genuine asymptotic expansions, the ratio with remainder $O(n^{-10/3})$.
\end{writenotejp}

\subsection{What the finite calculations do and do not establish}
Exact rational small-index tests check the original meander recurrence against the two-step operator, the diagonal normalization $r_n/n!$, the symmetrizer, the bidiagonal positive-semidefinite factorization, and the gauge contraction. These tests detect algebraic and indexing errors but do not establish asymptotic estimates.

Exact symbolic calculations check every formal recurrence coefficient through $t^9$, the boundary and derivative gauge, agreement of the triangular solver with an independently set-up generic linear system, and an independent endpoint-ratio conversion. The latter computes the ratio directly from $s_Ns_{N-1}F_N(0)/F_{N-2}(0)$, rather than reusing the integration of $\log s$. These certify finitely many polynomial identities. Proposition~\ref{air:jp:rb:prop:formal} proves their all-order continuation, and Section~\ref{air:jp:rb:sec:allorders} supplies the analytic transfer only for relaxed trees.

The integer dynamic program computes both counting triangles through $n=3000$ using exact integers and only a bounded number of rows. The archived checkpoint JSON stores high-precision derived diagnostics, not the exact integer count array itself. The script can regenerate the integer counts before evaluation. At $n=3000$, the uncorrected normalized quantities are approximately $205.00390917$ for relaxed trees and $213.44032023$ for compacted trees. Removing the six displayed log corrections gives approximately $166.9520924395$ and $173.1267049959$. These are diagnostic values, with no rigorous numerical error certificate for the limiting amplitudes.

\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{rcc}
\toprule
$n$ & Relaxed ratio residual & Compacted formal ratio residual\\
\midrule
500 & $1.274\cdot10^{-10}$ & $2.344\cdot10^{-10}$\\
1000 & $1.177\cdot10^{-11}$ & $2.146\cdot10^{-11}$\\
2000 & $1.106\cdot10^{-12}$ & $1.995\cdot10^{-12}$\\
3000 & $2.790\cdot10^{-13}$ & $5.000\cdot10^{-13}$\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
Here the residual is the absolute difference between the exact-count ratio evaluated at high precision and the displayed series truncated through $n^{-3}$. The observed decay corroborates the calculations; it is not a proof of the compacted expansion. Polynomial extrapolations using checkpoints from $1600$ through $3000$ give stable-looking values near $166.9520895742200$ and $173.1267048501473$. None of the extra digits is certified, and we do not use them anywhere in the proofs.

Frozen tridiagonal spectral diagnostics through $n=100000$ likewise agree with the scales in Proposition~\ref{air:jp:rb:prop:frozen}. For example, the scaled gap $(\lambda_n-\lambda_n^{(2)})n^{2/3}$ changes from about $6.1823$ at $n=100$ to $6.9897$ at $n=100000$, and $\sqrt n\phi_n(0)$ changes from $1.3665$ to $1.4137$, approaching $\sqrt2$. These are floating-point checks, separate from the min--max and smoothing arguments.
\begin{writenotejp}
Earlier evidence in this report: Part~\ref{air:part:rb} gives $166.9506$ and $166.9515$ at $n=20000$ after three corrections, Part~\ref{air:part:cb} gives $173.12105$ at $n=2500$, and the intake extrapolated $166.9508$ and $173.1263$ from exact counts to $n=900$. Report~100's six-correction values at $n=3000$ are closer to the published fits $166.95208957$ and $173.12670485$. The intake recomputed the ratio residuals of the table at $n=500$ and $n=1000$ from exact counts and found the same values, and the relaxed six-correction quotient $166.95227$ at $n=500$ and $166.95213$ at $n=1000$. None of these numbers is an enclosure.
\end{writenotejp}

\section{Rigorous inverse asymptotics}\label{air:jp:rb:sec:inverse}

Let $a_n$ denote $r_n$ with $\alpha=1$ or $c_n$ with $\alpha=3/4$. This section first uses only the already established $\Theta$ bounds \eqref{air:jp:rb:eq:theta}, so its first theorem applies to both sequences. The distinction between recovering the index of an exact sequence value and inverting an arbitrary threshold is essential.

\subsection{An eventual inverse requiring no amplitude value}
For $Y>0$ put
\begin{align}
 x_0(Y)&=\frac{Y}{W_0(4Y/e)},&L_0(Y)&=\log(4x_0(Y)),\label{air:jp:rb:eq:lambert}\\
 z_\alpha(Y)&=x_0(Y)-
 \frac{3ax_0(Y)^{1/3}+(\alpha+1/2)\log x_0(Y)+\tfrac12\log(2\pi)}{L_0(Y)},\label{air:jp:rb:eq:inversez}
\end{align}
where $W_0$ is the positive real branch of Lambert's $W$ function. All formulas are used only for large positive $Y$.

\begin{theorem}[Exact-value inverse from two-sided bounds]\label{air:jp:rb:thm:inverse}
For either counting sequence,
\begin{equation}\label{air:jp:rb:eq:inverseerror}
 z_\alpha(\log a_n)=n+O(1/\log n).
\end{equation}
In particular, rounding $z_\alpha(\log a_n)$ to the nearest integer returns $n$ for all sufficiently large $n$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Stirling's formula and \eqref{air:jp:rb:eq:theta} imply
\[
 \log a_n=G(n)+O(1),
\]
where
\[
 G(x)=x(\log(4x)-1)+3ax^{1/3}
      +(\alpha+1/2)\log x+\tfrac12\log(2\pi).
\]
Let $G_0(x)=x(\log(4x)-1)$ and $g(x)=G(x)-G_0(x)$. The Lambert identity gives $G_0(x_0)=Y$ and $G'_0(x_0)=L_0$. Set $h=-g(x_0)/L_0$, so $z_\alpha=x_0+h$ and $h=O(x_0^{1/3}/\log x_0)$. Taylor expansion gives
\[
 G(z_\alpha)-Y=g'(x_0)h+O(h^2/x_0)
 =O(x_0^{-1/3}/\log x_0).
\]
The omitted smaller terms are absorbed in this bound since
$g'(x)=ax^{-2/3}+(\alpha+1/2)/x$.
At $Y=\log a_n$, the mean value theorem and $G'(x)\sim\log x$ give \eqref{air:jp:rb:eq:inverseerror}; both $x_0$ and $z_\alpha$ are asymptotic to $n$. The error tends to zero, proving the rounding assertion.
\end{proof}

The theorem is eventual, and the unspecified constants in the $\Theta$ bounds do not supply an effective cutoff. In the numerical checks, at $n=3000$ the error $z_\alpha(\log a_n)-n$ is approximately $+0.55818$ for relaxed trees and $+0.56223$ for compacted trees. Nearest-integer recovery therefore still fails at that moderate index. The theorem is not a certified finite-range algorithm.

\subsection{Arbitrary thresholds and the required exact comparison}
First establish monotonicity separately. For relaxed trees, \eqref{air:jp:rb:eq:triangle} gives
\[
 r_n=R(n,n-1)\ge nr_{n-1}\quad(n\ge2).
\]
For compacted trees, nonnegativity of the counting triangle and \eqref{air:jp:rb:eq:compacted} give
\[
 c_n\ge nc_{n-1}-(n-2)c_{n-2}\ge2c_{n-1}\quad(n\ge2),
\]
inductively, beginning with $c_0=c_1=1$ and $c_2=3$. Both sequences are strictly increasing from index $1$ onward. Direct differentiation of \eqref{air:jp:rb:eq:inversez} gives
$z_\alpha'(Y)\sim1/\log x_0>0$.

\begin{corollary}[Two candidates for a threshold inverse]\label{air:jp:rb:cor:threshold}
For a sufficiently large real threshold $y$, let
\[
 k(y)=\max\{n:a_n\le y\},\qquad j_0=\operatorname{round}(z_\alpha(\log y)).
\]
Then $j_0\in\{k(y),k(y)+1\}$ and
\[
 k(y)=\begin{cases}
 j_0,&a_{j_0}\le y,\\
 j_0-1,&a_{j_0}>y.
 \end{cases}
\]
Thus one exact or rigorously certified comparison selects the floor inverse. The least index $j(y)$ with $a_{j(y)}\ge y$ is obtained by the analogous single comparison: return $j_0$ if $a_{j_0}\ge y$, and $j_0+1$ otherwise. Equality belongs to the first branch.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
With $k=k(y)$, monotonicity and Theorem~\ref{air:jp:rb:thm:inverse} put
$z_\alpha(\log y)$ between $k+o(1)$ and $k+1+o(1)$. For large $k$, nearest-integer rounding therefore lies in $\{k,k+1\}$. Monotonicity of $a_n$ proves the floor comparison rule, including equality. For the least-index rule, the same argument puts $j_0$ in $\{j(y)-1,j(y)\}$; at equality the exact-value rounding theorem applies. The stated single comparison then selects the least index.
\end{proof}
This rule also has an unspecified eventual cutoff. An input arbitrarily close to a sequence value needs an exact or certified comparison; unverified floating-point evaluation of an asymptotic series cannot settle which side of the threshold it lies on.

\subsection{Arbitrary-order relaxed inverses}
The all-order forward theorem now gives much sharper inverse remainders for relaxed trees, with the canonically defined $\gamma_r$. For $J\ge0$, set
\begin{equation}\label{air:jp:rb:eq:FJ}
 \mathcal F_J(x)=\log\Gamma(x+1)+x\log4+3ax^{1/3}+\log x+\log\gamma_r
                 +\sum_{j=1}^Jb_jx^{-j/3}.
\end{equation}
For large $x$, $\mathcal F_J'(x)\sim\log x>0$. Let $x_J(Y)$ be its unique sufficiently large real root of $\mathcal F_J(x)=Y$.

\begin{corollary}[Sharpened relaxed inverse]\label{air:jp:rb:cor:sharpinverse}
For every fixed $J\ge0$,
\begin{equation}\label{air:jp:rb:eq:sharpinverse}
 x_J(\log r_n)=n+O\!\left(\frac{n^{-(J+1)/3}}{\log n}\right).
\end{equation}
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
Taking logarithms in \eqref{air:jp:rb:eq:allorders} gives
$\log r_n=\mathcal F_J(n)+O(n^{-(J+1)/3})$. The mean value theorem and $\mathcal F_J'(x)\sim\log x$ give \eqref{air:jp:rb:eq:sharpinverse}.
\end{proof}
This is an unconditional mathematical statement with a positive, convergently represented constant; using an uncertified decimal approximation for that constant does not give a certified numerical inverse. One may compute the root by Newton iteration from the Lambert start \eqref{air:jp:rb:eq:lambert}, continuing until the numerical error is smaller than the desired inverse scale. A single truncated Newton step is not automatically accurate to arbitrary order. For compacted trees the same implication holds \emph{conditionally} if its corresponding forward expansion and positive amplitude are established; only Theorem~\ref{air:jp:rb:thm:inverse} and Corollary~\ref{air:jp:rb:cor:threshold} are unconditional for it here.
\begin{writenotejp}
The condition is now met: the compacted forward expansion with a positive amplitude is Report~101's Theorem~\ref{air:jp:cb:thm:allorders} and Part~\ref{air:part:cb}'s Theorem~\ref{air:cb:thm:main}, and Report~101 states the unconditional compacted inverse as Corollary~\ref{air:jp:cb:cor:sharpinverse}. The inverse statements of this section are instances of the transseries volume's theorems named in Part~0 (``Instances of results elsewhere in the repository''); no novelty is claimed for the inversion mechanics. Theorem~\ref{air:jp:rb:thm:inverse} and Corollary~\ref{air:jp:rb:cor:threshold} are second routes to Part~\ref{air:part:rb}'s amplitude-free locator (Section~\ref{air:rb:an-amplitude-free-locator-and-its-existing-provenance}) and threshold brackets (Section~\ref{air:rb:discrete-threshold-bracketing-and-the-rounding-barrier}), and the Newton count $K$ below is an explicit specialization of the $j$-step bound of Section~\ref{air:rb:two-newton-steps-are-a-simpler-practical-inverse}.
\end{writenotejp}


\subsection{A quantified finite Newton algorithm}
For each prescribed inverse order, one can replace the implicit root by an explicitly bounded number of Newton steps. With $Y\to\infty$, start at $x_0$ in \eqref{air:jp:rb:eq:lambert}, put $L=\log(4x_0)$, and iterate
\begin{equation}\label{air:jp:rb:eq:newton}
 x_{k+1}=x_k-\frac{\mathcal F_J(x_k)-Y}{\mathcal F_J'(x_k)},
 \qquad K=\lceil\log_2(J+4)\rceil-1.
\end{equation}
The derivative is
\[
 \mathcal F_J'(x)=\psi(x+1)+\log4+ax^{-2/3}+x^{-1}
             -\sum_{j=1}^J\frac j3b_jx^{-j/3-1},
\]
where $\psi$ is the digamma function. For fixed $J$, the $K$th iterate satisfies
\begin{equation}\label{air:jp:rb:eq:newtonorder}
 x_K(\log r_n)=n+O\!\left(n^{-(J+1)/3}/\log n\right).
\end{equation}
To prove the iteration count, let $x_*$ be the root of $\mathcal F_J(x)=Y$. The Lambert equation and the size of the Airy term give
$x_*-x_0=O(x_0^{1/3}/L)$. On $x_0\pm o(x_0)$,
$\mathcal F_J'\sim L$ and $\mathcal F_J''=O(1/x_0)$. Taylor's theorem bounds the exact Newton root errors $e_k=x_k-x_*$ by
\[
 |e_{k+1}|\le C|e_k|^2/(x_0L).
\]
The iterates stay in that neighborhood, and induction gives
\[
 e_k=O\!\left(\frac{x_0^{1-2^{k+1}/3}}{L^{2^{k+1}-1}}\right).
\]
The first two errors are $O(x_0^{-1/3}/L^3)$ and $O(x_0^{-5/3}/L^7)$. Since $2^{K+1}\ge J+4$, this root error is no larger than the model error in Corollary~\ref{air:jp:rb:cor:sharpinverse}, proving \eqref{air:jp:rb:eq:newtonorder}.

There is also a version using only a finite Stirling polynomial in place of $\log\Gamma$. Substitute
\[
 (x+\tfrac12)\log x-x+\tfrac12\log(2\pi)
 +\sum_{r=1}^{R}\frac{B_{2r}}{2r(2r-1)x^{2r-1}},
 \qquad R=\max\!\left(0,\left\lceil\frac{J-2}{6}\right\rceil\right),
\]
where $B_{2r}$ are Bernoulli numbers, and differentiate that expression consistently in every step. Its remainder is $O(x^{-2R-1})$ and $2R+1\ge(J+1)/3$, so its root differs from $x_*$ by at most the desired inverse error. The same $K$ steps therefore suffice. Each fixed order requires finitely many arithmetic operations, logarithms, real fractional powers, and one principal Lambert-$W$ evaluation, together with the constants $a,\gamma_r,b_j$ and Bernoulli numbers. This is an asymptotic algorithm with the true constants and sufficiently accurate evaluations; uncertified amplitude digits or unbounded floating-point errors do not provide a certified numerical implementation.

\section{Further questions and proof boundaries}\label{air:jp:rb:sec:questions}
\begin{writenotejp}
These questions, with those of Reports~101 and~103, are re-scoped in Section~\ref{air:jp:sec:questions}, the Part's register of open and answered questions.
\end{writenotejp}

\paragraph{Effective amplitude enclosures.}
The canonical product and series provide a route to rigorous numerical bounds, but effective constants for the spectral, drift, projection, and boundary estimates are still needed. A useful next goal is an interval computation of the finite part together with an explicit tail enclosure. It would turn the quoted numerical amplitude into certified digits and make inverse cutoffs effective.

\paragraph{Compacted trees.}
The subtraction in \eqref{air:jp:rb:eq:compactedwalk} obstructs the direct positive Jacobi construction. A positive multistate encoding that records the forbidden local configurations, or a stable replacement that preserves the scalar amplitude, could address this. Upper and lower positive comparison recurrences alone can leave different constants and do not prove an amplitude limit. No compacted amplitude theorem is claimed by this article.
\begin{writenotejp}
Answered without a positive encoding: Report~101 (Theorems~\ref{air:jp:cb:thm:main} and~\ref{air:jp:cb:thm:allorders}) and Part~\ref{air:part:cb} prove the compacted amplitude and every fixed order by treating the subtraction as signed memory whose scalar drift cancels. Part~\ref{air:part:cb} also has exact positive four-state, three-state and renewal encodings, with obstructions to a symmetric Jacobi reduction (the section after Section~\ref{air:cb:sec:statement}). A proof of the compacted amplitude through a positive encoding remains open (Question~\ref{air:jp:q:positive}).
\end{writenotejp}

\paragraph{Exponentially small Airy sectors.}
The gap suggests relaxation on a stretched-exponential scale related to other Airy eigenvalues. To construct a genuine transseries one would need evolving excited-mode amplitudes, control of mode coupling, and remainder bounds beyond every algebraic order. The all-order Poincar\'e result does not determine these data, and formal subtraction of its infinite series is not justified.

\paragraph{Coefficient growth and summability.}
The triangular recursion proves existence at every fixed order. It does not bound growth as the order tends to infinity. Determining a Gevrey class, optimal truncation scale, or a summability mechanism would require estimates uniform in the truncation order, which the present envelope argument intentionally does not supply.

\paragraph{Higher arity and related recurrences.}
The relaxed $k$-ary results in \cite{gdw} retain a $\Theta$ formulation. The present strategy suggests looking for an exact finite positive representation, a limiting operator with a simple ground state and gap, summable gauge losses, and an endpoint kernel estimate. Each ingredient must be proved for the new recurrence; universality of the Airy shape alone is insufficient.
\begin{writenotejp}
Answered for every fixed arity $k\ge3$ by Part~\ref{air:part:ka}, for relaxed trees, compacted trees and automata, with a fixed weighted norm in place of an exact symmetrization; uniformity in $k$ remains open there.
\end{writenotejp}

\section{Reproducibility and provenance}\label{air:jp:rb:sec:reproduce}

The companion source archive contains this standalone \LaTeX{} source, the compiled PDF, a build script, and a separate \texttt{checks/} directory. The latter includes exact rational and symbolic tests, finite baseline coefficient data, and optional floating-point diagnostics. Its README specifies the replay commands and dependencies. Checks fail with explicit exceptions rather than relying on Python assertions, and corrupted-input regression tests run in both normal and optimized Python modes. The exact checks and numerical diagnostics are clearly labeled; no numerical output is promoted to an analytic theorem.

The report builds with pdf\LaTeX{} and standard AMS, Latin Modern, geometry, microtype, hyperref, and fancyhdr packages. The exact Python checks use Python 3 and SymPy. Optional numerical diagnostics use mpmath, NumPy, and SciPy as specified in their README. The provided build and replay commands do not fetch external source files or modify a research repository. A fresh archive extraction is rebuilt and replayed as part of release validation. The delivered PDF is rendered and inspected page by page, including displayed equations, tables, references, and page boundaries.

Primary provenance is explicit: the counting recurrences, initial $\Theta$ theorem, earlier Airy ansatz, and original amplitude estimates are from \cite{epfw}; the April 2026 statement of the remaining boundary difficulty is from \cite{epflw}. The exact sequence identifiers are linked in \cite{oeis}. The original authors' Maple supplement is linked in \cite{maple}; it is not redistributed in this archive. The proof in Sections~\ref{air:jp:rb:sec:jacobi}--\ref{air:jp:rb:sec:allorders} is presented self-contained rather than as an invocation of an unverified general transfer theorem.

The accompanying research inspected the primary 2020 and April 2026 papers and related literature, and found no duplicate relaxed-amplitude result in the relevant indices and project summaries of the inspected repository snapshot. That bounded search is not an exhaustive worldwide priority search. No publication, repository edit, external submission, or certification by an independent journal is implied.
\begin{writenotejp}
The ``inspected repository snapshot'' is commit \texttt{4b874cea0} (Report~100's provenance record, shipped as \repopath{data/102-100-relaxed-PROVENANCE.json}). The sentence was true there and is stale now: Part~\ref{air:part:rb} of this report, placed in commit \texttt{34f1acd4b} on 2 October 2026 from an archive that arrived the same day, proves the same relaxed amplitude theorem, and Part~\ref{air:part:cb} the compacted one. The ``companion source archive'' is Report~100's delivered archive, which survives in commit \texttt{60f54ea06}; the README of this report lists the files shipped from it and their names here.
\end{writenotejp}

\addtocontents{toc}{\protect\smallskip\protect\noindent\textit{Report 101: compacted binary trees}\protect\par}
\bigskip\noindent{\Large\bfseries Report 101: compacted binary trees}\par\nopagebreak
\begin{partsource}
Bundle Report~101, archive \repopath{A254789_Positive_Airy_Amplitude_and_All_Orders_Source.zip}, main file \repopath{compacted_tree_amplitude/compacted_tree_amplitude.tex} (693 lines; 19-page PDF), titled ``A positive Airy amplitude for compacted binary trees: Signed memory stability with all order expansions and inverse asymptotics'', dated 2 October 2026, author line ``Research prepared for private review''. Printed in full except for the restatement of Report~100 in its Section~3, label prefix \texttt{air:jp:cb:}; its Sections 1--12 are Sections~\ref{air:jp:cb:sec:result}--\ref{air:jp:cb:sec:reproduce}.
\end{partsource}

\paragraph{Abstract of Report 101.}
\begin{quote}\small
Let $c_n$ count compacted binary trees of size $n$, OEIS A254789, and let $a_1$ be the largest zero of the Airy function. We prove
\[
 c_n=\gamma_c n!4^n e^{3a_1n^{1/3}}n^{3/4}\bigl(1+O(n^{-1/3})\bigr),\qquad 0<\gamma_c<\infty.
\]
The exact normalized recurrence contains negative memory and does not inherit the positive contraction of relaxed trees. We eliminate odd times, control the resulting two memory operators globally after a diagonal gauge, and normalize by the relaxed Jacobi eigenvalue minus $1/n$. Counting domination first gives an $O(n^{1/4})$ bound. An Airy spectral gap and a cancellation of the scalar memory drift then give an absolutely convergent amplitude. Boundary smoothing and the published two-sided bound make that amplitude strictly positive. A signed forced-memory lemma and compatible polynomial-Airy profiles prove a full Poincar\'e expansion to every fixed algebraic order. We give exact coefficient and inverse algorithms, a convergent amplitude representation, and reproducible finite certificates. The required relaxed Jacobi lemmas are stated precisely and are proved in the companion article, supplied unchanged with this report. Numerical amplitude digits are not certified; no exponentially small transseries sector or exhaustive literature-priority claim is made.
\end{quote}

\section{The counting problem and the result}\label{air:jp:cb:sec:result}
Set $C(n,0)=1$, and $C(n,m)=0$ outside $0\le m\le n$. For $n\ge m\ge1$,
\begin{equation}\label{air:jp:cb:eq:C}
 C(n,m)=(m+1)C(n-1,m)+C(n,m-1)-(m-1)C(n-2,m-1).
\end{equation}
A term with zero prefactor is taken to be zero, without evaluating an invalid entry. The diagonal $c_n=C(n,n)$ begins
\[
 1,1,3,15,111,1119,14487,230943,4395855,97608831,\ldots.
\]
This is the compacted binary-tree triangle of \cite[Proposition 2.11]{epfw}. These are ordered binary compressed trees; the left and right child positions are distinct. Let $a=a_1$ be the largest real zero of $\Ai$, with $\Ai''(z)=z\Ai(z)$ and decay at positive infinity.

\begin{theorem}[Positive amplitude]\label{air:jp:cb:thm:main}
There exists $\gamma_c\in(0,\infty)$ such that
\begin{equation}\label{air:jp:cb:eq:main}
 c_n=\gamma_c n!4^ne^{3an^{1/3}}n^{3/4}\left(1+O(n^{-1/3})\right).
\end{equation}
The constant admits the absolutely convergent representation in Section~\ref{air:jp:cb:sec:constant}.
\end{theorem}
\begin{theorem}[All algebraic orders]\label{air:jp:cb:thm:allorders}
There is one compatible sequence $d_1,d_2,\ldots\in\Q[a]$ such that, for every fixed integer $J\ge0$,
\begin{equation}\label{air:jp:cb:eq:allorders}
 c_n=\gamma_c n!4^ne^{3an^{1/3}}n^{3/4}
 \left(1+\sum_{j=1}^Jd_jn^{-j/3}+O(n^{-(J+1)/3})\right).
\end{equation}
The same positive $\gamma_c$ is used at every depth. The coefficients are computed by the exact polynomial recursion in Section~\ref{air:jp:cb:sec:formal}.
\end{theorem}

The previously established result is
\begin{equation}\label{air:jp:cb:eq:theta}
 c_n=\Theta\!\left(n!4^ne^{3an^{1/3}}n^{3/4}\right),
\end{equation}
proved in \cite[Theorem 1.1]{epfw}. That paper explicitly leaves amplitude existence unresolved; its numerical estimate $\gamma_c\approx173.12670485$ is not a certified value. It already develops a formal Airy ansatz and high-order extrapolation, so the formal ansatz itself is not claimed as new. The April 2026 revision of \cite[Section 10.1]{epflw} explains why its equality theorem does not automatically settle this tree problem: the walk can leave the boundary and its asymptotic initial condition is unknown.

Here the missing amplitude step is a signed-memory stability argument. It uses both the published bound \eqref{air:jp:cb:eq:theta} and the independently proved relaxed-tree Jacobi estimates of the companion article, Report~100 (Sections~\ref{air:jp:rb:sec:result}--\ref{air:jp:rb:sec:reproduce} here). The latter is a mathematical dependency, not an appeal to an unproved compacted analogue. Its unchanged PDF and source archive accompany this article. Its statements that compacted trees remain unresolved describe that earlier article's scope; the present companion supplies the additional argument. No numerical fit is used in either theorem above.
\begin{writenotejp}
Theorems~\ref{air:jp:cb:thm:main} and~\ref{air:jp:cb:thm:allorders} are a second route to Part~\ref{air:part:cb}'s Theorem~\ref{air:cb:thm:main} ($a$ here is Part~\ref{air:part:cb}'s $z$, $\gamma_c$ the same constant), by essentially the same mechanism in even time: counting domination gives an $O(n^{1/4})$ start, the transverse error decays, and the scalar memory drift $1/(4n)$ cancels into a delayed difference. Part~\ref{air:part:cb} works with the one-step delay and the normalization $R_N=P_NN^{-1/4}$ (Sections~\ref{air:cb:sec:delay}--\ref{air:cb:sec:formal}); here the memory has three lags and the normalization is $\kappa_n=\lambda_n-1/n$. Both routes take positivity from the published lower bound \eqref{air:jp:cb:eq:theta}. New in Report~101 relative to Part~\ref{air:part:cb}: the genuine coefficients $b_4,b_5,b_6$ of \eqref{air:jp:cb:eq:logexpansion}, the ratio expansion of Section~\ref{air:jp:cb:sec:coefficients}, and the product formula \eqref{air:jp:cb:eq:K}. The ``unchanged PDF and source archive'' of the companion are byte copies of Report~100's PDF and archive inside Report~101's delivery; they are not shipped here (Report~100 is printed above, and its archive survives in commit \texttt{60f54ea06}).
\end{writenotejp}

\section{The exact signed memory recurrence}\label{air:jp:cb:sec:memory}
For $N\ge M\ge0$ of the same parity put
\[
 E(N,M)=\frac{C((N+M)/2,(N-M)/2)}{((N+M)/2)!},
\]
and put $E=0$ outside this wedge or off parity. The recurrence is
\begin{align}
 E(N,M)&=\frac{N-M+2}{N+M}E(N-1,M-1)+E(N-1,M+1)\notag\\
 &\hspace{12mm}-\frac{2(N-M-2)}{(N+M)(N+M-2)}E(N-3,M-1),\label{air:jp:cb:eq:walk}\\
 E(N,0)&=E(N-1,1).
\end{align}
The first equation is for $M>0$ away from its finitely many startup singularities. Initialize those entries from \eqref{air:jp:cb:eq:C}; $E(0,0)=1$.
Define
\[
 u_n(k)=E(2n,2k),\quad 0\le k\le n,\qquad
 o_n(k)=E(2n-1,2k+1),\quad0\le k<n.
\]
All vectors below are zero-extended to $\ell^2(\mathbb N_0)$, and $c_n=n!u_n(0)$. Direct substitution gives
\begin{align}
 u_n(k)&=a_n(k)o_n(k-1)+o_n(k)-b_n(k)o_{n-1}(k-1),\label{air:jp:cb:eq:evenodd}\\
 o_n(k)&=p_n(k)u_{n-1}(k)+u_{n-1}(k+1)-q_n(k)u_{n-2}(k),\notag
\end{align}
with $u_n(0)=o_n(0)$ and
\begin{align*}
 a_n(k)&=\frac{n-k+1}{n+k},& b_n(k)&=\frac{n-k-1}{(n+k)(n+k-1)},\\
 p_n(k)&=\frac{n-k}{n+k},&q_n(k)&=\frac{n-k-2}{(n+k)(n+k-1)}.
\end{align*}
Eliminating both odd-time vectors, for $n\ge4$,
\begin{equation}\label{air:jp:cb:eq:even}
 u_n=A_nu_{n-1}-B_nu_{n-2}+F_nu_{n-3}.
\end{equation}
The forward operator is the relaxed-tree operator
\begin{align}
 (A_nw)(0)&=w(0)+w(1),\label{air:jp:cb:eq:A}\\
 (A_nw)(k)&=\frac{(n-k+1)^2}{(n+k)(n+k-1)}w(k-1)
       +\frac{2n-2k+1}{n+k}w(k)+w(k+1),\notag
\end{align}
for $1\le k\le n$. The memory operators are
\begin{align}
 (B_nw)(0)&=\frac{n-2}{n(n-1)}w(0),\label{air:jp:cb:eq:B}\\
 (B_nw)(k)&=P_n(k)w(k-1)+Q_n(k)w(k),\notag\\
 P_n(k)&=\frac{(n-k-1)(2n-2k+1)}{(n+k)(n+k-1)(n+k-2)},\notag\\
 Q_n(k)&=\frac{2n-2k-3}{(n+k)(n+k-1)},\notag\\
 (F_nw)(k)&=W_n(k)w(k-1),\label{air:jp:cb:eq:F}\\
 W_n(k)&=\frac{(n-k-1)(n-k-2)}{(n+k)(n+k-1)(n+k-2)(n+k-3)},\notag
\end{align}
with $(F_nw)(0)=0$. The input supports are $0,\ldots,n-2$ for $B_n$ and $0,\ldots,n-3$ for $F_n$.

For clarity, the five algebraic identities producing these coefficients are
\begin{align*}
 L_n(k)&=a_n(k)p_n(k-1),&D_n(k)&=a_n(k)+p_n(k),\\
 P_n(k)&=a_n(k)q_n(k-1)+b_n(k)p_{n-1}(k-1),\\
 Q_n(k)&=q_n(k)+b_n(k),&W_n(k)&=b_n(k)q_{n-1}(k-1).
\end{align*}
At row zero the exact equation is
\[
 u_n(0)=u_{n-1}(0)+u_{n-1}(1)-\frac{n-2}{n(n-1)}u_{n-2}(0).
\]
Apparent negative coefficients beyond the stated input supports multiply zero and are not entries of the actual matrices. All actual entries of $B_n,F_n$ are nonnegative; $P_n(n-1)=0$ and $W_n(n-2)=0$. The sign before $B_n$ in \eqref{air:jp:cb:eq:even} is nevertheless negative. We never infer positivity or contraction of the full compacted evolution from the entrywise signs of its individual matrices.

\section{The relaxed Jacobi estimates used here}\label{air:jp:cb:sec:import}
\begin{writenotejp}
Report~101 opens this section by restating, word for word in substance, Report~100's definitions of Section~\ref{air:jp:rb:sec:jacobi}: the symmetrizer $S_n$ \eqref{air:jp:rb:eq:S}, the symmetric positive semidefinite $J_n=S_n^{-1}A_nS_n=C_nC_n^{\mathsf T}$ with diagonal $1$ at zero and $(2n-2k+1)/(n+k)$ elsewhere, its top eigenvalue $\lambda_n$ and positive unit eigenvector $\phi_n$, $H_n=J_n/\lambda_n$, the gauge $R_n$ \eqref{air:jp:rb:eq:R} (with $R_1(0)=1$), $T_n=H_nR_n$ and $\Lambda_n=\prod_{j\le n}\lambda_j$. It is not reprinted. The one change of letter: Report~101 writes $j_n(k)$ for the off-diagonal entry of $J_n$ that Report~100 calls $b_n(k)$ \eqref{air:jp:rb:eq:b}, because $b_n(k)$ is an elimination coefficient in Section~\ref{air:jp:cb:sec:memory}. Lemma~\ref{air:jp:cb:lem:import} is kept, because the rest of Report~101 cites it display by display.
\end{writenotejp}

\begin{lemma}[Companion Jacobi estimates]\label{air:jp:cb:lem:import}
For all sufficiently large $n$, with constants independent of $n$,
\begin{align}
 \lambda_n&=4+4an^{-2/3}+6n^{-1}+O(n^{-4/3}),\label{air:jp:cb:eq:lambda}\\
 \norm{(I-\phi_n\phi_n^{\mathsf T})H_n}&\le1-cn^{-2/3},\label{air:jp:cb:eq:gap}\\
 0\le R_n&\le I,\qquad 1-R_n(k)\le Ck^2/n^2,\label{air:jp:cb:eq:Rbound}\\
 \norm{(k+1)^p\phi_n}&\le C_p n^{p/3},\qquad p\ge0\text{ fixed},\label{air:jp:cb:eq:moment}\\
 \norm{\phi_n-\phi_{n-1}}&\le C/n,\label{air:jp:cb:eq:drift}\\
 \norm{(I-R_n)\phi_n}+\norm{(I-R_n)\phi_{n-1}}&\le Cn^{-4/3},\label{air:jp:cb:eq:gauge}\\
 \norm{Z\phi_n-\phi_n}&\le Cn^{-1/3},\label{air:jp:cb:eq:shift}\\
 \phi_n(0)&=\sqrt2\,n^{-1/2}(1+O(n^{-2/3})),\label{air:jp:cb:eq:phiboundary}
\end{align}
where $(Zw)(k)=w(k-1)$ and $w(-1)=0$. Moreover, for $0\le l\le\lfloor n^{2/3}\rfloor$,
\begin{equation}\label{air:jp:cb:eq:smooth}
 \norm{\delta_0^{\mathsf T}T_n\cdots T_{n-l+1}}\le C(l+1)^{-3/4}.
\end{equation}
For the actual relaxed counting vector $d_n$, its normalization
$v_n^r=\Lambda_n^{-1}S_n^{-1}d_n$ satisfies $\norm{v_n^r}\le1$.
\end{lemma}

Here $d_n(k)=R(n+k,n-k)/(n+k)!$, where $R$ obeys \eqref{air:jp:cb:eq:C} without its subtraction term and with the same boundary conditions. These estimates are proved in Report~100's Sections 2--5 (Sections~\ref{air:jp:rb:sec:jacobi}--\ref{air:jp:rb:sec:smoothing} here), included as a dependency. The half-line energy bound \eqref{air:jp:rb:eq:gradient},
\[
 \sum_{k\ge0}|\phi_n(k+1)-\phi_n(k)|^2+2\phi_n(0)^2\le Cn^{-2/3},
\]
implies \eqref{air:jp:cb:eq:shift}, including both zero-extension endpoints. We apply \eqref{air:jp:cb:eq:smooth} only to the relaxed propagator, never to the signed compacted product.
\begin{writenotejp}
Between the first and the last sentence of this paragraph Report~101 summarizes how Report~100 proves the lemma: min--max convergence of $n^{2/3}(4I-J_n)$ to the Dirichlet operator $-\partial_x^2+8x$ with ground state $\sqrt2\Ai(2x+a)/\Ai'(a)$ on the half grid $x_k=(k+\frac12)n^{-1/3}$, the quasimode residual that gives $6/n$, exponential barriers for the moments, the gap applied to $(J_n-J_{n-1})\phi_{n-1}$ for the drift, and the killed lazy-walk kernel $P$ with $P^m(0,0)\le C(m+1)^{-3/2}$ for \eqref{air:jp:cb:eq:smooth}; it adds that the companion ``retains the full variational, residual, boundary, and localization proofs''. That summary repeats Sections~\ref{air:jp:rb:sec:airy}--\ref{air:jp:rb:sec:smoothing} and is not reprinted; the derivation of \eqref{air:jp:cb:eq:shift}, which Report~100 does not state, is kept. Each line of the lemma is a line of Report~100: \eqref{air:jp:cb:eq:lambda} is \eqref{air:jp:rb:eq:eigen}; \eqref{air:jp:cb:eq:gap} follows from \eqref{air:jp:rb:eq:gap} because $J_n$ is positive semidefinite; \eqref{air:jp:cb:eq:Rbound} is Lemma~\ref{air:jp:rb:lem:gauge}; \eqref{air:jp:cb:eq:drift} and \eqref{air:jp:cb:eq:phiboundary} are \eqref{air:jp:rb:eq:drift} and \eqref{air:jp:rb:eq:endpoint}, and \eqref{air:jp:cb:eq:moment} follows from \eqref{air:jp:rb:eq:moments} since $(k+1)^p\le2^p(k^p+1)$; \eqref{air:jp:cb:eq:gauge} is \eqref{air:jp:rb:eq:gauge-vector}; \eqref{air:jp:cb:eq:smooth} is \eqref{air:jp:rb:eq:smoothing}; and $\norm{v_n^r}\le1$ is the contraction stated after \eqref{air:jp:rb:eq:product}. Report~101 ships the companion's PDF and source archive unchanged (byte copies of Report~100's archive); they are not shipped here, since Report~100 is printed in full above.
\end{writenotejp}

\section{Global bounds for the two gauged memory operators}\label{air:jp:cb:sec:gauged}
Put $\widetilde B_n=S_n^{-1}B_nS_{n-2}$ and $\widetilde F_n=S_n^{-1}F_nS_{n-3}$.
\begin{lemma}\label{air:jp:cb:lem:memorybounds}
On their entire finite input supports,
\begin{align}
 \norm{\widetilde B_n}&\le C/n,&\norm{\widetilde F_n}&\le C/n^2,\label{air:jp:cb:eq:globalmemory}\\
 \norm{\widetilde B_n\phi_{n-2}-(4/n)\phi_n}&\le Cn^{-4/3}.\label{air:jp:cb:eq:memoryaction}
\end{align}
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The diagonal gauge ratio is $S_{n-2}(k)/S_n(k)=R_n(k)R_{n-1}(k)\le1$. On the lower band,
\[
 \frac{S_{n-2}(k-1)}{S_n(k)}=\frac{R_n(k-1)R_{n-1}(k-1)}{j_n(k)}.
\]
Hence the corresponding gauged entry is bounded by
\[
 \frac{P_n(k)}{j_n(k)}=
 \frac{n-k-1}{n-k+1}\,
 \frac{2n-2k+1}{(n+k-2)\sqrt{(n+k)(n+k-1)}}\le\frac Cn.
\]
The diagonal bound follows from $Q_n$ and its exceptional first row. For $\widetilde F_n$ there is one further factor $R_{n-2}(k-1)\le1$, and
\[
 \frac{W_n(k)}{j_n(k)}=
 \frac{(n-k-1)(n-k-2)}{n-k+1}\,
 \frac1{(n+k-2)(n+k-3)\sqrt{(n+k)(n+k-1)}}\le\frac C{n^2}.
\]
These estimates include $k$ close to $n$: the small Jacobi edge is canceled by the displayed numerators. Summing the norms of the one or two diagonal bands proves \eqref{air:jp:cb:eq:globalmemory}.

For the finer assertion, on inputs supported at $0,\ldots,n-2$ write
\begin{equation}\label{air:jp:cb:eq:Bapprox}
 \widetilde B_n=\frac2n(I+Z)-\frac1n\Pi_0+V_n,
\end{equation}
where $\Pi_0$ projects onto coordinate zero. Each band of $V_n$ has row-$k$ entry bounded by $C(k+1)/n^2$. On $k\le n/2$, the explicit rational and square-root formulas give
$P_n(k)/j_n(k)=2/n+O((k+1)/n^2)$ and $Q_n(k)=2/n+O((k+1)/n^2)$, uniformly. The gauge products differ from one by $O(k^2/n^2)$, adding only $O(k^2/n^3)$. At row zero the true entry is $1/n+O(n^{-2})$, explaining $-\Pi_0/n$. For $k>n/2$, the crude band bounds already imply the claimed $C(k+1)/n^2$ error, including the zero upper-edge entries.

For $\psi=\phi_{n-2}$, the moment bound gives $\norm{V_n\psi}\le Cn^{-5/3}$. Also $\norm{Z\psi-\psi}\le Cn^{-1/3}$ and $\norm{\Pi_0\psi}\le\norm{Z\psi-\psi}$. Applying \eqref{air:jp:cb:eq:Bapprox} gives $\widetilde B_n\psi=(4/n)\psi+O_{\ell^2}(n^{-4/3})$. Finally $\norm{\phi_n-\psi}\le C/n$ by two applications of \eqref{air:jp:cb:eq:drift}, proving \eqref{air:jp:cb:eq:memoryaction}.
\end{proof}

\section{Normalization and convergence of the scalar amplitude}\label{air:jp:cb:sec:tracking}
Set
\begin{equation}\label{air:jp:cb:eq:kappa}
 \kappa_n=\lambda_n-1/n,\qquad\Gamma_n=\prod_{j=1}^n\kappa_j,\qquad
 v_n=\Gamma_n^{-1}S_n^{-1}u_n.
\end{equation}
Every $\kappa_n$ is positive: $\lambda_1=3/2$, and for $n\ge2$ the Rayleigh quotient at $\delta_0$ gives $\lambda_n\ge1>1/n$. Thus
\[
 \kappa_n=4+4an^{-2/3}+5/n+O(n^{-4/3}).
\]
Define
\[
 M_n=\frac{\widetilde B_n}{\kappa_n\kappa_{n-1}},\quad
 N_n=\frac{\widetilde F_n}{\kappa_n\kappa_{n-1}\kappa_{n-2}},\quad
 \rho_n=\frac{\lambda_n}{\kappa_n}.
\]
Equation \eqref{air:jp:cb:eq:even} becomes exactly
\begin{equation}\label{air:jp:cb:eq:normalized}
 v_n=\rho_nT_nv_{n-1}-M_nv_{n-2}+N_nv_{n-3}.
\end{equation}
The estimates already proved imply
\begin{align}
 \rho_n&=1+\frac1{4n}+O(n^{-5/3}),\label{air:jp:cb:eq:normalizedbounds}\\
 \norm{M_n}&\le C/n,\quad\norm{N_n}\le C/n^2,\notag\\
 \norm{M_n\phi_{n-2}-(1/(4n))\phi_n}&\le Cn^{-4/3}.\notag
\end{align}

\subsection{A comparison bound that starts the bootstrap}
Compacted decorated paths form a subset of relaxed decorated paths. Equivalently, \cite[Lemma 5.1 and its proof]{epfw} gives $0\le u_n\le d_n$ coordinatewise. The positive diagonal gauge preserves this order, so
\[
 0\le v_n\le(\Lambda_n/\Gamma_n)v_n^r.
\]
Moreover,
\[
 \log\frac{\Lambda_n}{\Gamma_n}
 =\sum_{j=1}^n-\log\left(1-\frac1{j\lambda_j}\right)
 =\tfrac14\log n+O(1),
\]
because $1/(j\lambda_j)=1/(4j)+O(j^{-5/3})$. Since $\norm{v_n^r}\le1$,
\begin{equation}\label{air:jp:cb:eq:polynomial}
 \norm{v_n}\le Cn^{1/4}.
\end{equation}
This is a bound on the actual counting solution. It assumes no positive or contractive compacted operator on arbitrary initial data.

\subsection{Perpendicular tracking}
Write
\[
 a_n=\ip{\phi_n}{v_n},\quad w_n=v_n-a_n\phi_n,\quad\beta_n=\norm{w_n},
 \quad Q_n^\perp=I-\phi_n\phi_n^{\mathsf T}.
\]
The counting solution has $a_n\ge0$. Gauge contraction and Perron drift imply
\[
 \norm{Q_n^\perp R_n\phi_{n-1}}\le C/n,\qquad
 \norm{Q_n^\perp R_nw_{n-1}}\le\beta_{n-1}.
\]
Since $H_n$ commutes with $Q_n^\perp$, the gap absorbs $\rho_n=1+O(1/n)$ and gives, for all large $n$,
\begin{align}
 \beta_n\le{}&(1-c'n^{-2/3})\beta_{n-1}
 +\frac Cn|a_{n-1}|+\frac Cn\norm{v_{n-2}}+\frac C{n^2}\norm{v_{n-3}}.\label{air:jp:cb:eq:betarecurrence}
\end{align}
By \eqref{air:jp:cb:eq:polynomial} the forcing is $O(n^{-3/4})$, hence
\begin{equation}\label{air:jp:cb:eq:betafirst}
 \beta_n=O(n^{-1/12}).
\end{equation}
We use the elementary scalar comparison: if $x_n\le(1-cn^{-2/3})x_{n-1}+Cn^{-r}$, with $r>2/3$, then $x_n=O(n^{2/3-r})$. A sufficiently large multiple of that power is a supersolution for all large $n$, because its change between adjacent indices is smaller than its damping term; enlarge it to cover the finite prefix.

\subsection{The scalar memory cancellation}
Projection of \eqref{air:jp:cb:eq:normalized}, using $H_n\phi_n=\phi_n$, gives
\begin{equation}\label{air:jp:cb:eq:projection}
 a_n=\rho_n\ip{R_n\phi_n}{v_{n-1}}
       -\ip{\phi_n}{M_nv_{n-2}}+\ip{\phi_n}{N_nv_{n-3}}.
\end{equation}
Before cancellation, $\norm{R_n\phi_n-\phi_{n-1}}\le C/n$ and \eqref{air:jp:cb:eq:polynomial} imply
\begin{equation}\label{air:jp:cb:eq:crudescale}
 \Delta a_n:=a_n-a_{n-1}=O(n^{-3/4}).
\end{equation}
For the sharper estimate,
\[
 \ip{\phi_n}{R_n\phi_{n-1}}-1=O(n^{-4/3}),
\]
since its $R_n$-free part is $-\norm{\phi_n-\phi_{n-1}}^2/2=O(n^{-2})$ and the rest is bounded by \eqref{air:jp:cb:eq:gauge}. Therefore
\begin{align*}
 \rho_n\ip{\phi_n}{R_n\phi_{n-1}}-1&=\frac1{4n}+O(n^{-4/3}),\\
 \ip{\phi_n}{M_n\phi_{n-2}}&=\frac1{4n}+O(n^{-4/3}).
\end{align*}
The perpendicular part of the first term in \eqref{air:jp:cb:eq:projection} is
\[
 \rho_n\ip{R_n\phi_n-\phi_{n-1}}{w_{n-1}}=O(\beta_{n-1}/n).
\]
The remaining two perpendicular terms use the global norms of $M_n,N_n$. Thus the exact scalar equation has the form
\begin{equation}\label{air:jp:cb:eq:delta}
 \Delta a_n=\frac1{4n}\Delta a_{n-1}+\eta_n,
\end{equation}
where
\begin{align}
 |\eta_n|\le{}&Cn^{-4/3}(|a_{n-1}|+|a_{n-2}|)+Cn^{-2}|a_{n-3}|\notag\\
 &+\frac Cn(\beta_{n-1}+\beta_{n-2})+\frac C{n^2}\beta_{n-3}.\label{air:jp:cb:eq:eta}
\end{align}
Insert $a_n=O(n^{1/4})$, \eqref{air:jp:cb:eq:betafirst}, and \eqref{air:jp:cb:eq:crudescale}. The largest term is $O(n^{-13/12})$, while the delayed difference contributes $O(n^{-7/4})$. Hence $\sum_n|\Delta a_n|<\infty$ and $a_n\to a_\infty\ge0$.

Now $a_n$ is bounded and $\beta_n\to0$, so $v_n$ is bounded. A second application of \eqref{air:jp:cb:eq:betarecurrence} gives $\beta_n=O(n^{-1/3})$. The crude scalar estimate improves to $\Delta a_n=O(1/n)$; equations \eqref{air:jp:cb:eq:delta}--\eqref{air:jp:cb:eq:eta} then give $\Delta a_n=O(n^{-4/3})$. Summing the tail proves
\begin{equation}\label{air:jp:cb:eq:tracking}
 |a_n-a_\infty|+\norm{v_n-a_\infty\phi_n}\le Cn^{-1/3}.
\end{equation}
The two bootstraps are sequential, not circular. The cancellation in \eqref{air:jp:cb:eq:delta} removes the nonsummable $1/(4n)$ scalar drift; this is the decisive effect of the negative memory.

\section{Boundary smoothing and the positive amplitude}\label{air:jp:cb:sec:constant}
Put $z_n=v_n-a_\infty\phi_n$. The exact rewrite around the relaxed propagator is
\begin{align*}
 z_n&=T_nz_{n-1}+g_n,\\
 g_n&=(\rho_n-1)T_nv_{n-1}-M_nv_{n-2}+N_nv_{n-3}
       +a_\infty(T_n\phi_{n-1}-\phi_n).
\end{align*}
Boundedness of $v_n$ and the previous estimates imply $\norm{g_n}\le C/n$. The forcing may have either sign. Iterate over $L=\lfloor n^{2/3}\rfloor$ steps and use the row-norm estimate \eqref{air:jp:cb:eq:smooth}:
\begin{align*}
 |z_n(0)|&\le C(L+1)^{-3/4}n^{-1/3}
       +\frac Cn\sum_{l=0}^{L-1}(l+1)^{-3/4}\\
 &=O(n^{-5/6}).
\end{align*}
Thus
\begin{equation}\label{air:jp:cb:eq:endpoint}
 v_n(0)=\sqrt2\,a_\infty n^{-1/2}+O(n^{-5/6}).
\end{equation}

Since $\log(\kappa_n/4)=an^{-2/3}+\tfrac54n^{-1}+O(n^{-4/3})$, summation gives
\begin{equation}\label{air:jp:cb:eq:product}
 \Gamma_n=K_c4^ne^{3an^{1/3}}n^{5/4}(1+O(n^{-1/3})),\qquad K_c\in(0,\infty).
\end{equation}
One exact convergent representation is
\begin{equation}\label{air:jp:cb:eq:K}
 K_c=\frac{\kappa_1}{4e^{3a}}
 \prod_{j=2}^\infty
 \frac{\kappa_j}{4e^{3a(j^{1/3}-(j-1)^{1/3})}(j/(j-1))^{5/4}}.
\end{equation}
Its logarithmic factors are $O(j^{-4/3})$, and their tail is $O(n^{-1/3})$.

Divide the published positive lower bound \eqref{air:jp:cb:eq:theta} by $n!\Gamma_n$. It yields $v_n(0)\ge c n^{-1/2}$ eventually. If $a_\infty=0$, \eqref{air:jp:cb:eq:endpoint} instead gives $O(n^{-5/6})$, a contradiction. Therefore $a_\infty>0$. Because $S_n(0)=1$ and $c_n=n!\Gamma_nv_n(0)$, Theorem~\ref{air:jp:cb:thm:main} follows with
\begin{equation}\label{air:jp:cb:eq:gamma}
 \gamma_c=\sqrt2K_ca_\infty,\qquad
 a_\infty=a_3+\sum_{n=4}^\infty(a_n-a_{n-1}).
\end{equation}
The series is absolutely convergent and all its entries are specified by the exact normalized recurrence and the finite Perron eigensystems. These formulas characterize the constant without an amplitude-limit hypothesis. They do not give certified decimal digits without explicit numerical tail bounds.
\begin{writenotejp}
This is the one external analytic input of Report~101, as of Part~\ref{air:part:cb}, whose endpoint section uses the same lower bound \eqref{air:cb:eq:known-theta}. Report~100's intrinsic positivity argument, Lemma~\ref{air:jp:rb:lem:positive}, does not transfer verbatim, because the signed three-lag evolution \eqref{air:jp:cb:eq:normalized} is not entrywise nonnegative; see Question~\ref{air:jp:q:intrinsic}. The product \eqref{air:jp:cb:eq:K} and the series in \eqref{air:jp:cb:eq:gamma} are the compacted counterparts of Report~100's Theorem~\ref{air:jp:rb:thm:representation}; Part~\ref{air:part:cb} expresses $\gamma_c$ through the one-step product instead, \eqref{air:cb:eq:gamma}.
\end{writenotejp}

\section{A forced signed memory lemma}\label{air:jp:cb:sec:forced}
The next result turns arbitrarily accurate approximate solutions into genuine asymptotics. It applies to signed errors and does not use the counting comparison again.
\begin{lemma}[Forced memory transfer]\label{air:jp:cb:lem:forced}
Suppose finite-support vectors $z_n$ satisfy
\begin{equation}\label{air:jp:cb:eq:forced}
 z_n=\rho_nT_nz_{n-1}-M_nz_{n-2}+N_nz_{n-3}+f_n,
\end{equation}
where $\norm{f_n}=O(n^{-p})$, $p>4/3$, and $\norm{z_n}=O(n^{-1/3})$. Then
\begin{equation}\label{air:jp:cb:eq:forcedconclusion}
 \norm{z_n}=O(n^{-(p-1)}),\qquad z_n(0)=O(n^{-p+1/2}).
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Put $\alpha_n=\ip{\phi_n}{z_n}$ and $b_n=\norm{(I-\phi_n\phi_n^{\mathsf T})z_n}$. The initial norm hypothesis implies $\alpha_n\to0$. Suppose presently $\norm{z_n}=O(n^{-q})$, initially $q=1/3$. Perpendicular projection as in \eqref{air:jp:cb:eq:betarecurrence} gives
\[
 b_n\le(1-cn^{-2/3})b_{n-1}+O(n^{-q-1})+O(n^{-p}),
\]
so the scalar comparison implies
\begin{equation}\label{air:jp:cb:eq:forcedbeta}
 b_n=O(n^{-q-1/3}+n^{-p+2/3}).
\end{equation}
The uncancelled scalar projection gives $\Delta\alpha_n=O(n^{-q-1}+n^{-p})$. The refined scalar identity is
\[
 \Delta\alpha_n=\frac1{4n}\Delta\alpha_{n-1}+\eta_n+\ip{\phi_n}{f_n},
\]
with \eqref{air:jp:cb:eq:eta} using $\alpha,b$ in place of $a,\beta$. The delayed difference is $O(n^{-q-2}+n^{-p-1})$; the other errors are bounded by \eqref{air:jp:cb:eq:forcedbeta}. Therefore
\[
 \Delta\alpha_n=O(n^{-q-4/3}+n^{-p}).
\]
These terms are summable because $q\ge1/3$ and $p>4/3$. Sum from $n+1$ to infinity using $\alpha_\infty=0$ to get
\[
 \alpha_n=O(n^{-q-1/3}+n^{-p+1}).
\]
Together with \eqref{air:jp:cb:eq:forcedbeta}, this improves $q$ to $\min(q+1/3,p-1)$. Finitely many iterations reach exactly $p-1$, proving the norm assertion.

Rewrite \eqref{air:jp:cb:eq:forced} as $z_n=T_nz_{n-1}+h_n$, where
\[
 h_n=(\rho_n-1)T_nz_{n-1}-M_nz_{n-2}+N_nz_{n-3}+f_n.
\]
The improved norm gives $\norm{h_n}=O(n^{-p})$. Smoothing over $L=\lfloor n^{2/3}\rfloor$ steps gives
\[
 |z_n(0)|\le CL^{-3/4}n^{-(p-1)}
  +Cn^{-p}\sum_{l=0}^{L-1}(l+1)^{-3/4}
 =O(n^{-p+1/2})+O(n^{-p+1/6}),
\]
as claimed. No sign is required of $z_n,f_n$, or $h_n$.
\end{proof}

\section{An exact polynomial Airy coefficient algorithm}\label{air:jp:cb:sec:formal}
Use $\varkappa=2^{2/3}a$, distinct from the even-time normalization $\kappa_n$. Set
\[
 t=N^{-1/3},\quad x=(M+1)t,\quad A(x)=\Ai(a+2^{1/3}x),\quad v(x)=2x+\varkappa.
\]
Then $A''=vA$, $A(0)=0$, and $A'(0)\ne0$; primes here differentiate with respect to $x$.
Seek formal series
\begin{align}
 s(t)&=2+\varkappa t^2+\sum_{j\ge3}\sigma_jt^j,\label{air:jp:cb:eq:formals}\\
 F(t,x)&=\sum_{j\ge0}t^jf_j(x),\qquad f_j=P_jA+Q_jA',\label{air:jp:cb:eq:formalF}
\end{align}
where $P_j,Q_j\in\Q[\varkappa,x]$, $P_0=1,Q_0=0$, with gauges
\begin{equation}\label{air:jp:cb:eq:formalgauge}
 Q_j(0)=0,\qquad P_j(0)+Q_j'(0)=0\quad(j\ge1).
\end{equation}
These ensure $f_j(0)=f_j'(0)=0$ for $j\ge1$, while $f_0=A$ fixes the normalization.
Let $t_j=t(1-jt^3)^{-1/3}$. The exact formal equation is
\begin{align}
 s(t)F(t,x)={}&W(t,x)F(t_1,(x-t)(1-t^3)^{-1/3})\notag\\
 &+F(t_1,(x+t)(1-t^3)^{-1/3})\notag\\
 &-\frac{\mathcal B(t,x)}{s(t_1)s(t_2)}
 F(t_3,(x-t)(1-3t^3)^{-1/3}),\label{air:jp:cb:eq:formalrec}
\end{align}
where
\[
 W=\frac{1-xt^2+3t^3}{1+xt^2-t^3},\qquad
 \mathcal B=\frac{2t^3(1-xt^2-t^3)}{(1+xt^2-t^3)(1+xt^2-3t^3)}.
\]
It is obtained from \eqref{air:jp:cb:eq:walk} by dividing through the preceding scalar carrier. In particular the two inverse factors in the last term are essential.

\begin{proposition}[Existence and uniqueness at every order]\label{air:jp:cb:prop:formal}
There is a unique compatible formal solution \eqref{air:jp:cb:eq:formals}--\eqref{air:jp:cb:eq:formalrec} satisfying \eqref{air:jp:cb:eq:formalgauge}, and all its coefficients belong to the displayed rational polynomial rings. Its first scalar correction is $\sigma_3=13/6$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
The powers $t^0,t^1$ cancel identically and $t^2$ gives $A''-(2x+\varkappa)A=0$. Suppose the coefficients through degree $t^{k-1}$ have vanished, for $k\ge3$. Differentiation preserves polynomial pairs times $A,A'$. Let the known coefficient at $t^k$ be $R_0A+R_1A'$. The new unknown profile $PA+QA'$ and coefficient $\sigma_k$ enter as
\[
 (\partial_x^2-v)(PA+QA')-\sigma_kA+R_0A+R_1A'=0.
\]
The delayed term starts at $t^3$, so it contains neither new unknown at this order. Equating the two polynomial coefficients gives
\begin{align*}
 P''+2vQ'+2Q-\sigma_k+R_0&=0,\\
 2P'+Q''+R_1&=0.
\end{align*}
Elimination of $P$ yields
\[
 \mathcal TQ=2R_0-R_1'-2\sigma_k,\qquad
 \mathcal TQ=Q'''-4vQ'-4Q.
\]
On polynomials of degree at most $d$, $\mathcal T$ is triangular with diagonal entries $-4(2j+1)$ for $j=0,\ldots,d$. It is invertible and preserves $\Q[\varkappa,x]$. Set
\begin{align*}
 \widetilde q&=\mathcal T^{-1}(2R_0-R_1'),&
 \sigma_k&=-2\widetilde q(0),&Q&=\widetilde q+\sigma_k/2,\\
 P(x)&=\int_0^x\frac{-R_1(u)-Q''(u)}2\,du-Q'(0).
\end{align*}
These formulas give both differential identities and both gauges, and conversely those identities and gauges determine the same solution. The recursion extends indefinitely. Direct evaluation of its first stage gives $\sigma_3=13/6$. This is a finite-dimensional exact construction at each order, not a claim that the infinite series converges.
\end{proof}

\section{Compatible profiles with arbitrarily small genuine residuals}\label{air:jp:cb:sec:profiles}
\subsection{A scalar carrier with one common constant}
Define coefficients $\ell_j\in\Q[\varkappa]$ by the formal identity
\begin{align}
 \log(s(t)/2)={}&\frac{3\varkappa}{2t}\bigl(1-(1-t^3)^{1/3}\bigr)
 -\frac{13}{12}\log(1-t^3)\notag\\
 &+\sum_{j\ge1}\ell_jt^j\bigl(1-(1-t^3)^{-j/3}\bigr).\label{air:jp:cb:eq:carrierrec}
\end{align}
The $t^2,t^3$ coefficients match because they are $\varkappa/2$ and $13/12$. At degree $j+3$, the new $\ell_j$ has coefficient $-j/3\ne0$, so it is uniquely determined. Fix the leading multiplicative constant to one at every depth. For integer $D\ge2$ let
\begin{align}
 H_N^{[D]}&=2^N\exp\!\left(\tfrac{3\varkappa}2N^{1/3}\right)N^{13/12}
  \exp\!\left(\sum_{j=1}^D\ell_jN^{-j/3}\right),\label{air:jp:cb:eq:H}\\
 F^{[D]}(t,x)&=\sum_{j=0}^Dt^jf_j(x).
\end{align}
The exact ratio $H_N^{[D]}/H_{N-1}^{[D]}$ matches $s(t)$ through degree $D+3$. Computing these carrier coefficients may use more formal profiles than retained in $F^{[D]}$; Proposition~\ref{air:jp:cb:prop:formal} supplies them. Arbitrary products of truncated ratios with independently chosen initial constants must not replace \eqref{air:jp:cb:eq:H}, since that could change the amplitude with $D$.

In even time put $\eps=n^{-1/3}$, $X=(k+1/2)\eps$, so $t=2^{-1/3}\eps$ and $x=2^{2/3}X$. Define the uncut approximation
\begin{equation}\label{air:jp:cb:eq:U}
 U_n^{[D]}(k)=\frac{2^{-13/12}}{\Ai'(a)}H_{2n}^{[D]}F^{[D]}(t,x).
\end{equation}
The derivative in this prefactor is the usual Airy-argument derivative. Its leading term is
\[
 \frac{4^ne^{3an^{1/3}}n^{13/12}}{\Ai'(a)}\Ai(a+2X).
\]
At $k=0$, $\Ai(a+\eps)/\Ai'(a)=\eps+O(\eps^3)$, hence
\begin{equation}\label{air:jp:cb:eq:Uendpoint}
 U_n^{[D]}(0)=4^ne^{3an^{1/3}}n^{3/4}\bigl(1+O(\eps)\bigr).
\end{equation}
Taylor expansion produces a compatible relative power series through any fixed degree not exceeding $D$. A newly included profile $\eps^jf_j(x)$ vanishes at $x=0$, so at $X_0=\eps/2$ it contributes only $O(\eps^{j+1})$ before division by the leading $\eps$. A newly included scalar term starts at relative degree $j$. Thus deeper truncations leave earlier endpoint coefficients unchanged and preserve leading coefficient one.

\subsection{A uniform envelope for the delayed recurrence}
For every fixed $D,C_0$, the exact one-step residual of $H_N^{[D]}F^{[D]}$, divided by $H_N^{[D]}$, is bounded uniformly for $0\le M\le C_0\sqrt N$ by
\begin{equation}\label{air:jp:cb:eq:one-step-residual}
 Ct^{D+3}(1+x)^A e^{-cx^{3/2}},
\end{equation}
for constants $C,A,c>0$ depending on the fixed depth. Here $x\le Ct^{-1/2}$, so $|xt^2|\le Ct^{3/2}$ and the denominators $1+xt^2-jt^3$ are uniformly separated from zero. Finite rational Taylor expansions therefore have remainders bounded by prescribed powers of $t$ times polynomials in $x$. The finitely many exact carrier quotients $H_{N-j}^{[D]}/H_N^{[D]}$, $j\le3$, are bounded and have Taylor expansions with bounded remainders, directly from \eqref{air:jp:cb:eq:H}. The shifted arguments differ from $x$ by $\pm t+O(xt^3+t^4)=O(t)$.

Every fixed derivative of a polynomial-Airy profile is again a polynomial pair times $A,A'$, bounded by $C(1+x)^Ae^{-cx^{3/2}}$. The same envelope, with changed constants, holds over the Taylor intervals. Multiplication preserves such envelopes. Formal cancellation removes powers through $t^{D+2}$, and the matching carrier ratios leave \eqref{air:jp:cb:eq:one-step-residual}. The negative memory coefficient begins at $t^3$ and loses no order.

At $M=0$, both terms at the ghost height $M-1=-1$ vanish exactly because every retained $f_j(0)=0$. Thus the estimate holds for the actual exceptional boundary $E(N,0)=E(N-1,1)$. No approximate boundary condition has been substituted.

\subsection{Exact residual elimination and cutoff at every lag}
Define odd-time approximants from the same expression $H_N^{[D]}F^{[D]}$ with the same prefactor $2^{-13/12}/\Ai'(a)$ as in \eqref{air:jp:cb:eq:U}. Let $r_{e,n},r_{o,n}$ denote their even and odd residuals in \eqref{air:jp:cb:eq:evenodd}. Let $K_n$ map $o_n$ to $u_n$ with diagonal $1$ and lower diagonal $a_n(k)$, and let $G_n$ map $o_{n-1}$ to $u_n$ with lower diagonal $b_n(k)$. Direct substitution gives the exact identity
\begin{align}
 U_n^{[D]}-A_nU_{n-1}^{[D]}+B_nU_{n-2}^{[D]}-F_nU_{n-3}^{[D]}
 =r_{e,n}+K_nr_{o,n}-G_nr_{o,n-1}.\label{air:jp:cb:eq:residual-elimination}
\end{align}
The coefficients of $K_n$ are bounded and those of $G_n$ are $O(1/n)$ in the region under consideration. Consecutive carriers have bounded ratios. Therefore the pointwise residual order $\eps^{D+3}$ survives this elimination. At row zero $K_n$ reads coordinate zero and $G_n$ is zero, so the same identity covers the boundary.

Choose a fixed smooth function $\chi$, equal to one on $[0,1/2]$ and zero on $[1,\infty)$, and put
\[
 \widetilde U_n^{[D]}(k)=\chi(k/\sqrt n)U_n^{[D]}(k).
\]
Whenever neighboring-height or lagged cutoff factors at $n,n-1,n-2,n-3$ differ, $k$ is comparable to $\sqrt n$ and $X$ to $n^{1/6}$. Every profile multiplied by a bounded carrier ratio is then at most a polynomial in $n$ times $e^{-cn^{1/4}}$. Each cutoff defect is smaller than every fixed inverse power after carrier normalization; cancellation among these defects is unnecessary. The residual support is $k\le\sqrt n+O(1)$.

Write $D_n^{\rm bulk}=4^ne^{3an^{1/3}}n^{13/12}$. The $X$-lattice spacing is $\eps$ and the squared envelope sums to $O(\eps^{-1})$. Hence
\begin{align}
 &\norm{\widetilde U_n^{[D]}-A_n\widetilde U_{n-1}^{[D]}
                 +B_n\widetilde U_{n-2}^{[D]}-F_n\widetilde U_{n-3}^{[D]}}
 =O(D_n^{\rm bulk}\eps^{D+5/2}).\label{air:jp:cb:eq:rawresidual}
\end{align}
On this support $S_n^{-1}$ is uniformly bounded, since its factorial product gives $-\log S_n(k)=O(k^2/n+k/n)=O(1)$. Also $D_n^{\rm bulk}/\Gamma_n=O(n^{-1/6})=O(\eps^{1/2})$. Consequently
\begin{align}
 &\norm{\Gamma_n^{-1}S_n^{-1}
 \left[\widetilde U_n^{[D]}-A_n\widetilde U_{n-1}^{[D]}
                 +B_n\widetilde U_{n-2}^{[D]}-F_n\widetilde U_{n-3}^{[D]}\right]}
 =O(\eps^{D+3}).\label{air:jp:cb:eq:normalizedresidual}
\end{align}
This is a genuine global norm estimate for every fixed depth, not merely pointwise formal cancellation.

\subsection{The leading profile and the all order transfer}
The unit leading Airy sample is $q_n(k)=\sqrt{2\eps}\Ai(a+2X)/\Ai'(a)$, up to normalization $1+O(\eps^2)$, with $\norm{\phi_n-q_n}=O(\eps^2)$ by the companion proof. Equations \eqref{air:jp:cb:eq:product} and \eqref{air:jp:cb:eq:U} give
\begin{equation}\label{air:jp:cb:eq:leadingapprox}
 \norm{\Gamma_n^{-1}S_n^{-1}\widetilde U_n^{[D]}
           -\frac1{\sqrt2K_c}\phi_n}=O(\eps).
\end{equation}
Indeed the finite scalar corrections are $1+O(\eps)$; higher fixed profiles have relative weighted norm $O(\eps)$; and $S_n^{-1}-1$ is $O(\eps(1+X^2))$ in that norm by the bounded logarithm estimate. The cutoff tail is exponentially small. The leading constant in \eqref{air:jp:cb:eq:leadingapprox} is independent of $D$.

Now let
\[
 z_n=v_n-\gamma_c\Gamma_n^{-1}S_n^{-1}\widetilde U_n^{[D]}.
\]
Since $\gamma_c=\sqrt2K_ca_\infty$, equations \eqref{air:jp:cb:eq:tracking} and \eqref{air:jp:cb:eq:leadingapprox} imply $\norm{z_n}=O(n^{-1/3})$. Equation \eqref{air:jp:cb:eq:normalizedresidual} supplies the forcing in Lemma~\ref{air:jp:cb:lem:forced}, with $p=(D+3)/3>4/3$. Its endpoint error, multiplied by $\Gamma_n/[4^ne^{3an^{1/3}}n^{3/4}]=\Theta(n^{1/2})$, is $O(n^{-(p-1)})=O(n^{-D/3})$. Given any desired $J$, choose $D\ge\max(2,J+1)$. Combine this with the compatible endpoint expansion of \eqref{air:jp:cb:eq:Uendpoint}. This proves Theorem~\ref{air:jp:cb:thm:allorders}, with one positive amplitude at every order.

\section{Explicit coefficients and the scope of finite certificates}\label{air:jp:cb:sec:coefficients}
The coefficient ring in Theorem~\ref{air:jp:cb:thm:allorders} follows from a grading, not merely the finite examples below. Let $\omega^3=1$, and let $B_{\varkappa}$ solve $B''=(2x+\varkappa)B$ with $B(0)=0,B'(0)=1$. Uniqueness of this initial-value problem gives
\[
 B_{\omega\varkappa}(\omega x)=\omega B_{\varkappa}(x).
\]
Both $W$ and $\mathcal B$ in \eqref{air:jp:cb:eq:formalrec} are invariant under $(t,x,\varkappa)\mapsto(\omega t,\omega x,\omega\varkappa)$. Uniqueness of the formal recursion with its boundary and derivative gauges therefore makes $s(t)$ and the normalized endpoint $F(t,t)/(tA'(0))$ invariant. The carrier equation \eqref{air:jp:cb:eq:carrierrec}, with its zero scalar integration constant, preserves the same invariance. Hence a scalar monomial $\varkappa^rt^j$ has $r+j$ divisible by three. Substituting $\varkappa=2^{2/3}a$ and $t=2^{-1/3}\eps$ gives $2^{(2r-j)/3}a^r\eps^j$, with an integer exponent of two. All endpoint coefficients thus belong to $\Q[a]$.

It is convenient to use logarithmic coefficients $b_j$, defined by the genuine expansion
\begin{align}
 \log c_n={}&\log(n!)+n\log4+3an^{1/3}+\tfrac34\log n+\log\gamma_c
       +\sum_{j=1}^Jb_jn^{-j/3}+O(n^{-(J+1)/3}).\label{air:jp:cb:eq:logexpansion}
\end{align}
The first six are
\begin{align*}
 b_1&=\frac{53a^2}{90},&
 b_2&=\frac{271a}{216},\\
 b_3&=-\frac{41728a^3-477495}{1360800},&
 b_4&=-\frac{2053a^2}{9720},\\
 b_5&=\frac{a(2869124a^3-329492565)}{1377810000},&
 b_6&=-\frac{320744a^3+10924065}{132269760}.
\end{align*}
They are exact rational polynomials in the Airy zero. Exponentiating gives, for example,
\begin{align*}
 d_1&=\frac{53a^2}{90},\\
 d_2&=\frac{a(2809a^3+20325)}{16200},\\
 d_3&=\frac{2084278a^6+43365690a^3+21487275}{61236000}.
\end{align*}
Further multiplicative coefficients through degree six and normalized diagonal-ratio coefficients through degree nine are included in the exact JSON certificates. The ratio begins
\[
 \frac{c_n}{4n c_{n-1}}=
 1+an^{-2/3}+\frac34n^{-1}+\frac{41a^2}{135}n^{-4/3}
  +\frac{20a}{81}n^{-5/3}+O(n^{-2}).
\]
To obtain a ratio remainder of a prescribed order, use the all-order forward expansion deeper than that order before division. Dividing two shallow big-$O$ formulas does not justify extra cancellation of their remainders.

The finite formal certificate verifies both polynomial components of every recurrence coefficient through $t^9$, exact boundary and derivative gauges, an independent generic linear-system construction, endpoint integration, and an independent conversion of log corrections into diagonal ratios. It contains both relaxed and compacted data because the same algebraic engine applies to both. The compacted analytic transfer proved above is additional to these finite algebraic identities. Finite tests alone cannot establish asymptotic convergence or amplitude positivity.

The numerical estimate $173.12670485$ is inherited from \cite{epfw}. At $n=3000$, exact integer dynamic programming followed by high-precision evaluation gives the uncorrected normalization approximately $213.44032023$; removing six logarithmic corrections gives approximately $173.1267049959$. This slow convergence is consistent with $b_1n^{-1/3}$ but is not a certified enclosure for $\gamma_c$. The optional floating-point gauged recurrence independently agrees with the exact-integer diagonal diagnostics to about $4\cdot10^{-12}$ relative error at $n=3000$. None of these decimal comparisons is used in the proof.
\begin{writenotejp}
With $a=z$, the coefficients $b_1,b_2,b_3$ are Part~\ref{air:part:cb}'s logarithmic corrections \eqref{air:cb:eq:log-corrections} ($477495/1360800=393/1120$), and $d_1,d_2,d_3$ are its relative coefficients $\mathfrak c_1,\mathfrak c_2,\mathfrak c_3$. The genuine $b_4,b_5,b_6$ are new to this report; they are the formal $b^{(c)}_4,b^{(c)}_5,b^{(c)}_6$ of Report~100's \eqref{air:jp:rb:eq:bcompacted}, now proved. The ratio display stops at $n^{-5/3}$; Report~100's formal compacted ratio, printed after \eqref{air:jp:rb:eq:bcompacted}, continues it through $n^{-3}$ and is genuine by Corollary~\ref{air:jp:cor:compacted}. The conversions $b\mapsto d$ and $b\mapsto$ ratio were recomputed symbolically at the write (difference zero). The grading argument is Part~\ref{air:part:cb}'s (Section~\ref{air:cb:sec:coefficients}), in this Part's variables. The ``exact JSON certificates'' are those of Report~100's suite, which Report~101 ships as a byte copy (not shipped again here; Report~100's copies are \repopath{data/102-100-relaxed-checks-data-formal_9.json} and \repopath{data/102-100-relaxed-checks-data-endpoint_9.json}).
\end{writenotejp}

\section{Rigorous inverse asymptotics}\label{air:jp:cb:sec:inverse}
The distinction between recovering the index of an exact count and inverting an arbitrary threshold matters. Throughout this section $\alpha=3/4$, and all statements are eventual as their arguments tend to infinity.
\begin{writenotejp}
Theorem~\ref{air:jp:cb:thm:inverse} and Corollary~\ref{air:jp:cb:cor:threshold} are Report~100's Theorem~\ref{air:jp:rb:thm:inverse} and Corollary~\ref{air:jp:rb:cor:threshold} at $\alpha=3/4$, with the same proofs (Report~100 states them for both sequences); they are printed again because Report~101's Newton algorithm below starts from its own \eqref{air:jp:cb:eq:lambert}. Corollary~\ref{air:jp:cb:cor:sharpinverse} is the unconditional form of the compacted inverse that Report~100 states only conditionally. All of it is a second route to Part~\ref{air:part:cb}'s inverse section (Section~\ref{air:cb:sec:inverse}) and an instance of the transseries volume's inversion theorems (Part~0).
\end{writenotejp}

\subsection{A Lambert start that does not need the amplitude value}
For large $Y>0$ define
\begin{align}
 x_0(Y)&=\frac{Y}{W_0(4Y/e)},&L_0(Y)&=\log(4x_0(Y)),\label{air:jp:cb:eq:lambert}\\
 z(Y)&=x_0(Y)-\frac{3ax_0(Y)^{1/3}+(5/4)\log x_0(Y)+\tfrac12\log(2\pi)}{L_0(Y)},\label{air:jp:cb:eq:z}
\end{align}
where $W_0$ is the positive real branch of Lambert's $W$ function.
\begin{theorem}[Eventual exact-value recovery]\label{air:jp:cb:thm:inverse}
One has $z(\log c_n)=n+O(1/\log n)$. Thus nearest-integer rounding recovers $n$ for all sufficiently large exact sequence values $c_n$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Stirling's formula and \eqref{air:jp:cb:eq:theta} give $\log c_n=G(n)+O(1)$, where
\[
 G(x)=x(\log(4x)-1)+3ax^{1/3}+\tfrac54\log x+\tfrac12\log(2\pi).
\]
Let $G_0(x)=x(\log(4x)-1)$ and $g=G-G_0$. The Lambert identity gives $G_0(x_0)=Y$ and $G_0'(x_0)=L_0$. With $h=-g(x_0)/L_0=O(x_0^{1/3}/\log x_0)$, Taylor expansion yields
\[
 G(x_0+h)-Y=g'(x_0)h+O(h^2/x_0)=O(x_0^{-1/3}/\log x_0),
\]
because $g'(x)=ax^{-2/3}+5/(4x)$. At $Y=\log c_n$, the mean value theorem and $G'(x)\sim\log x$ imply the assertion; $x_0,z,n$ are asymptotic. The error tends to zero, so eventual rounding is valid.
\end{proof}
The unknown constants in the two-sided bound do not supply an effective cutoff. At $n=3000$, $z(\log c_n)-n$ is approximately $0.56223$, so nearest-integer recovery still fails there. This theorem is not a certified finite-range inversion algorithm.

\subsection{Arbitrary thresholds require one exact comparison}
The counting sequence is strictly increasing from index one onward. Indeed \eqref{air:jp:cb:eq:C} and nonnegativity give, inductively,
\[
 c_n\ge nc_{n-1}-(n-2)c_{n-2}\ge2c_{n-1}\quad(n\ge2),
\]
starting with $c_0=c_1=1,c_2=3$. Also direct differentiation of \eqref{air:jp:cb:eq:z} gives $z'(Y)\sim1/\log x_0>0$.
\begin{corollary}[Threshold inversion]\label{air:jp:cb:cor:threshold}
For sufficiently large $y$, let $k(y)=\max\{n:c_n\le y\}$ and $j_0=\operatorname{round}(z(\log y))$. Then $j_0\in\{k(y),k(y)+1\}$ and
\[
 k(y)=\begin{cases}j_0,&c_{j_0}\le y,\\j_0-1,&c_{j_0}>y.\end{cases}
\]
For the least index $j(y)$ with $c_{j(y)}\ge y$, return $j_0$ if $c_{j_0}\ge y$ and $j_0+1$ otherwise. Equality belongs to the first branch in each rule.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
Monotonicity and Theorem~\ref{air:jp:cb:thm:inverse} put $z(\log y)$ between $k(y)+o(1)$ and $k(y)+1+o(1)$. Eventual nearest-integer rounding therefore gives only the two stated candidates, and comparison selects the correct one. The least-index rule is analogous; at equality the exact-value theorem applies.
\end{proof}
A threshold arbitrarily close to a sequence value requires an exact or rigorously certified comparison. Unverified floating-point asymptotics do not determine its side of that value.

\subsection{Arbitrary order inverse remainders}
For fixed $J\ge0$, put
\begin{equation}\label{air:jp:cb:eq:FJ}
 \mathcal F_J(x)=\log\Gamma(x+1)+x\log4+3ax^{1/3}+\tfrac34\log x
       +\log\gamma_c+\sum_{j=1}^Jb_jx^{-j/3}.
\end{equation}
Here $\Gamma(x+1)$ is the usual gamma function, distinct from the product $\Gamma_n$ in \eqref{air:jp:cb:eq:kappa}. Its derivative satisfies $\mathcal F_J'(x)\sim\log x>0$. Let $x_J(Y)$ be the unique sufficiently large solution of $\mathcal F_J(x)=Y$.
\begin{corollary}[All order compacted inverse]\label{air:jp:cb:cor:sharpinverse}
For every fixed $J$,
\begin{equation}\label{air:jp:cb:eq:sharpinverse}
 x_J(\log c_n)=n+O\!\left(\frac{n^{-(J+1)/3}}{\log n}\right).
\end{equation}
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
Equation \eqref{air:jp:cb:eq:logexpansion} gives $\log c_n=\mathcal F_J(n)+O(n^{-(J+1)/3})$. Apply the mean value theorem with $\mathcal F_J'(x)\sim\log x$.
\end{proof}
This is an unconditional mathematical consequence of the all-order theorem, with the canonically specified positive constant $\gamma_c$. Replacing it by uncertified digits does not make a certified numerical inverse.

\subsection{A finite Newton algorithm at each prescribed order}
Start at \eqref{air:jp:cb:eq:lambert}, put $L=\log(4x_0)$, and iterate
\begin{equation}\label{air:jp:cb:eq:newton}
 x_{k+1}=x_k-\frac{\mathcal F_J(x_k)-Y}{\mathcal F_J'(x_k)},
 \qquad K=\lceil\log_2(J+4)\rceil-1.
\end{equation}
The derivative is
\[
 \mathcal F_J'(x)=\psi(x+1)+\log4+ax^{-2/3}+\frac3{4x}
     -\sum_{j=1}^J\frac j3b_jx^{-j/3-1},
\]
where $\psi$ is the digamma function. The $K$th iterate satisfies the same inverse error \eqref{air:jp:cb:eq:sharpinverse}. To prove the iteration count, let $x_*$ be the root of $\mathcal F_J(x)=Y$. The Airy term gives $x_*-x_0=O(x_0^{1/3}/L)$. On $x_0\pm o(x_0)$, $\mathcal F_J'\sim L$ and $\mathcal F_J''=O(1/x_0)$. Thus exact Newton errors obey
\[
 |e_{k+1}|\le C|e_k|^2/(x_0L),\qquad
 e_k=O\!\left(\frac{x_0^{1-2^{k+1}/3}}{L^{2^{k+1}-1}}\right).
\]
The iterates remain in that neighborhood. Since $2^{K+1}\ge J+4$, the root error is no larger than $O(n^{-(J+1)/3}/\log n)$, proving the assertion. A single unqualified Newton step does not give arbitrary-order accuracy.

There is a version with finitely many elementary operations in place of the gamma function. Substitute the finite Stirling expression
\[
 (x+\tfrac12)\log x-x+\tfrac12\log(2\pi)
 +\sum_{r=1}^{R}\frac{B_{2r}}{2r(2r-1)x^{2r-1}},
 \qquad R=\max\!\left(0,\left\lceil\frac{J-2}{6}\right\rceil\right),
\]
for $\log\Gamma(x+1)$, and differentiate that same expression in every Newton step. Its remainder is $O(x^{-2R-1})$ and $2R+1\ge(J+1)/3$, so the root changes by no more than the required inverse error. The same $K$ steps suffice. At each fixed order this uses finitely many arithmetic operations, logarithms, real fractional powers, and one principal Lambert-$W$ evaluation, with constants $a,\gamma_c,b_j$ and Bernoulli numbers. Effective certified evaluation also needs amplitude enclosures and numerical error control, neither supplied by the asymptotic statement alone.

\section{Reproducibility and limits of the result}\label{air:jp:cb:sec:reproduce}
The accompanying source archive contains this article's \texttt{.tex} source, a deterministic three-pass build script, explicit exact checks, the formal coefficient certificates and verifier, and the unchanged relaxed companion PDF and source archive. The latter provides the complete proofs behind Lemma~\ref{air:jp:cb:lem:import}. It is a private mathematical companion prepared for this project, not an externally peer-reviewed publication.

Run \texttt{./build.sh} to rebuild this PDF and \texttt{./replay.sh} to replay the certificates. The build rejects overfull boxes or unresolved references. The new compacted verifier checks exact one-step elimination identities symbolically, the complete even-time recurrence on exact rational counting arrays, upper-boundary supports, squared gauge ratios, and the canceled small-edge identities. The supplied formal verifier reconstructs the profiles with a triangular solver and compares them to an independent linear-system solution, then checks all local recurrence coefficients through order nine and endpoint coefficient families.

Every acceptance gate raises an explicit exception and remains active under \texttt{python -O}. Deliberate negative cases include disabled or reduced coverage, altered boundary or memory coefficients, damaged gauge identities, malformed coefficient data, a boundary-preserving formal perturbation, and corrupted endpoint certificates. The exact replay runs in normal and optimized modes; negative tests must be rejected for the intended reasons. The release manifest records sizes and SHA-256 hashes. The archive is extracted to a fresh directory for a full rebuild and replay before delivery, and all rendered PDF pages are visually inspected.

The distinction between proof and certificate is important. These finite checks validate exact identities, indexing, coefficient computations, and reproducibility. The convergence, uniform residuals, nonzero amplitude, and all-order error estimates are the analytic arguments in this article and its supplied dependency. No finite matrix sweep or numerical fit proves those statements.
\begin{writenotejp}
These paragraphs describe Report~101's delivered archive (commit \texttt{60f54ea06}). Its build script, release manifest and manuscript are not shipped; its formal verifier, certificates and negative tests are byte copies of Report~100's and are shipped once, under Report~100's prefix. The ``private mathematical companion'' is Report~100, printed in this Part; Reports~100 and~101 both name their author as ``Research prepared for private review''. The README of this report lists the shipped files.
\end{writenotejp}

\paragraph{What remains open here.}
The canonical constant is positive and convergently represented, but its decimal digits and an effective inverse cutoff are not certified. Explicit constants in the spectral, drift, tracking, and endpoint bounds would permit interval tail enclosures. The finite-order expansion does not bound coefficient growth uniformly in the order, establish a Gevrey class, justify optimal truncation, or determine exponentially small Airy sectors. A full transseries requires separate control of those terms and their amplitudes. No such conclusion follows merely from an expansion valid to every fixed algebraic order.
\begin{writenotejp}
Re-scoped in Section~\ref{air:jp:sec:questions}: certified digits and effective cutoffs (Question~\ref{air:jp:q:certified}), coefficient growth and Gevrey bounds (Question~\ref{air:jp:q:gevrey}), exponentially small sectors (Question~\ref{air:jp:q:sectors}).
\end{writenotejp}

\addtocontents{toc}{\protect\smallskip\protect\noindent\textit{Report 103: minimal binary automata}\protect\par}
\bigskip\noindent{\Large\bfseries Report 103: minimal binary automata}\par\nopagebreak
\begin{partsource}
Bundle Report~103, archive \repopath{A331120_Minimal_Binary_Automata_All_Orders_Source.zip}, main file \repopath{minimal_dfa_amplitude/minimal_dfa_amplitude.tex} (927 lines; 23-page PDF), titled ``A positive Airy amplitude for minimal binary automata: Signed memory and all order asymptotics for OEIS A331120'', dated 2 October 2026, author line ``Research prepared for Vladimir''. Printed in full except for the restatement of Report~100 in its Section~3, label prefix \texttt{air:jp:dfa:}; its Sections 1--13 are Sections~\ref{air:jp:dfa:sec:result}--\ref{air:jp:dfa:sec:reproduction}.
\end{partsource}

\paragraph{Abstract of Report 103.}
\begin{quote}\small
Let $b_n$ count non-isomorphic minimal deterministic finite automata with $n$ transient states over a fixed binary alphabet, recognizing a finite language. The unique sink gives $n+1$ total states. Starting from the exact counting triangle of Elvey Price, Fang, and Wallner, we prove
\[
 b_n=\gamma_D n!8^n e^{3an^{1/3}}n^{7/8}
 \left(1+\sum_{j=1}^{m}c_jn^{-j/3}+O(n^{-(m+1)/3})\right)
\]
for every fixed $m\ge0$, with one common constant $0<\gamma_D<\infty$; $a$ is the largest zero of the Airy function. The signed recurrence has two delayed correction terms. A precise cancellation of its nonsummable scalar drift permits Perron tracking, and a published compacted-tree lower bound rules out a zero amplitude. We then construct profiles to arbitrary depth, prove uniform residual estimates at the exact boundary and all memory lags, and transfer them to the actual counting solution. Four explicit correction coefficients, a convergent amplitude characterization, and arbitrary-order Lambert--Newton inverse estimates are given. The required relaxed-tree spectral theorem is stated completely and imported from the immutable companion proof enclosed with the source package. No convergent infinite asymptotic series, exponentially small sectors, closed-form amplitude, certified amplitude digits, or literature-priority claim is asserted.
\end{quote}

\section{The result and its published starting point}\label{air:jp:dfa:sec:result}
The sequence is OEIS A331120 \cite{oeis}:
\[
 (b_n)_{n\ge0}=1,1,6,60,900,18480,487560,15824880,612504240,\ldots.
\]
Our indexing follows the counting triangle in Proposition 5 of Elvey Price--Fang--Wallner \cite{dfa}. The term $n=0$ is its initial convention; all asymptotic statements concern $n\to\infty$. Put
\[
 a=\max\{x\in\mathbb R:\Ai(x)=0\}=-2.338107410459\ldots.
\]
The numerical value identifies the root only; the constant in every theorem is the exact Airy zero.

\begin{theorem}[Positive amplitude and all algebraic orders]\label{air:jp:dfa:thm:main}
There is a constant $\gamma_D\in(0,\infty)$ and a unique compatible sequence of real coefficients $(c_j)_{j\ge1}$ such that for each fixed integer $m\ge0$,
\begin{equation}\label{air:jp:dfa:eq:main}
 b_n=\gamma_D n!8^ne^{3an^{1/3}}n^{7/8}
 \left[1+\sum_{j=1}^{m}c_jn^{-j/3}+O(n^{-(m+1)/3})\right].
\end{equation}
The constant in the error may depend on $m$. Equivalently, there are compatible logarithmic coefficients $d_j$ such that
\begin{equation}\label{air:jp:dfa:eq:logmain}
 \log\frac{b_n}{\gamma_D n!8^ne^{3an^{1/3}}n^{7/8}}
 =\sum_{j=1}^{m}d_jn^{-j/3}+O(n^{-(m+1)/3}).
\end{equation}
They are produced by the exact polynomial algorithm of Section~\ref{air:jp:dfa:sec:formal}. The amplitude is characterized by convergent quantities in Section~\ref{air:jp:dfa:sec:amplitude}.
\end{theorem}

The published theorem \cite[Theorem 1]{dfa} already establishes the sharp scale
